\sup_{v\in S^{d-1}}\int\exp(s|\langle x,v\rangle|)d\mu(x)\leq\exp(4\sigma^{2}s^{2}).

We obtain an immediate corollary:

Corollary B.8
.

Suppose
\mu
is
\sigma
-subgaussian in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
and
z\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Then:

\int\exp(|\langle x,z\rangle|)d\mu(x)\leq\exp(4\tilde{C}_{G}^{2}\sigma^{2}\|z\|^{2}+\|\mathbb{E}(\mu)\|\|z\|).

where
\tilde{C}_{G}
is an absolute constant.

Proof.

For any
z
,

\displaystyle\int\exp(|\langle x,z\rangle|)d\mu(x)=

\displaystyle\int\exp(|\langle x-\mathbb{E}[\mu]+\mathbb{E}[\mu],z\rangle|)d\mu(x)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int\exp(|\langle x-\mathbb{E}[\mu],z\rangle|+|\langle\mathbb{E}[\mu],z\rangle|)d\mu(x)

(30)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp(|\langle\mathbb{E}[\mu],z\rangle|)\int\exp(|\langle x-\mathbb{E}[\mu],z\rangle|)d\mu(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp(|\langle\mathbb{E}[\mu],z\rangle|)\int\exp\left(\left|\left\langle x-\mathbb{E}[\mu],\frac{z}{\|z\|}\right\rangle\right|\|z\|\right)d\mu(x)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp(\|\mathbb{E}[\mu]\|\|z\|)\sup_{v\in S^{d-1}}\int\exp(|\langle x-\mathbb{E}[\mu],v\rangle|\|z\|)d\mu(x)

(31)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp(4\|\tilde{\mu}\|^{2}_{\mathcal{G}}\;\|z\|^{2}+\|\mathbb{E}[\mu]\|\|z\|).

(32)

where
\tilde{\mu}
is the distribution of the mean zero random variable
X-\mathbb{E}[\mu]
. In (
30
) we applied the triangle inequality, in (
31
) we applied the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, and (
32
) follows from Lemma
B.7
. We conclude by applying Lemma
A.6
to
\tilde{\mu}
, giving us a final bound of
\exp(4\tilde{C}_{G}^{2}\sigma^{2}\|z\|^{2}+\|\mathbb{E}[\mu]\|\|z\|)
.
∎

Lemma B.9
.

(Lemma 4.9 from
[
43
]
) Let
\mu,\nu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, and choose entropic potentials that solve (
2
) such that
\int f^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}d\mu\geq 0
and
\int g^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}d\nu\geq 0
. Then:

\inf_{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\{\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}-g^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}(y)\right\}\leq f^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}(x)\leq\frac{1}{2}\int\|x-y\|^{2}d\nu(y),

\inf_{x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}}\left\{\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}-f^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}(x)\right\}\leq g^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}(y)\leq\frac{1}{2}\int\|x-y\|^{2}d\mu(x),

for all
x,y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}.

The lower bounds in Lemma
B.9
, while general, can be difficult to apply. We derive more straightforward bounds in the special case when one of the measures is subgaussian. The lemma below can be extracted from Proposition A.1 in
[
39
]
.

Lemma B.10
.

Let
\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
\nu\in\mathcal{G}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, and choose entropic potentials that solve (
2
) such that
\int f^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}d\mu\geq 0
and
\int g^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}d\nu\geq 0
. Then there exists a constant
C_{\epsilon,\sigma,\|\mathbb{E}(\mu)\|,M_{2}(\mu),\|\mathbb{E}(\nu)\|}\geq 0
, depending quadratically on
\sigma
,
\|\mathbb{E}(\mu)\|
, and linearly on
{M}_{2}(\mu),\frac{1}{\epsilon}
and
\|\mathbb{E}(\nu)\|
such that for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
:

-C_{\epsilon,\sigma,\|\mathbb{E}(\mu)\|,M_{2}(\mu),\|\mathbb{E}(\nu)\|}\left(\|x\|^{2}+1\right)\leq f^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}(x).

Proof.

By (
3
), we may write
-f^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}(x)=\displaystyle\epsilon\log\int\exp\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}(g^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}(y)-\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2})\right)d\nu(y)
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. We then bound:

\displaystyle-f^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\epsilon\log\int\exp\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\left(g^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}(y)-\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}\right)\right)d\nu(y)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\epsilon\log\int\exp\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\left(\frac{1}{2}\int\|z-y\|^{2}d\mu(z)-\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}\right)\right)d\nu(y)

(33)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\epsilon\log\int\exp\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\left(\frac{1}{2}M_{2}(\mu)-\langle y,\mathbb{E}(\mu)\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\|y\|^{2}-\frac{1}{2}\|x\|^{2}+\langle x,y\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\|y\|^{2}\right)\right)d\nu(y)

(34)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\epsilon\log\int\exp\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\left(\frac{1}{2}M_{2}(\mu)-\langle y,\mathbb{E}(\mu)-x\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\|x\|^{2}\right)\right)d\nu(y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}M_{2}(\mu)-\frac{1}{2}\|x\|^{2}+\epsilon\log\int\exp\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\left(-\langle y,\mathbb{E}(\mu)-x\rangle\right)\right)d\nu(y)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}M_{2}(\mu)-\frac{1}{2}\|x\|^{2}+\epsilon\log\int\exp\left(\frac{1}{\epsilon}\left|\langle y,\mathbb{E}(\mu)-x\right\rangle|\right)d\nu(y)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}M_{2}(\mu)-\frac{1}{2}\|x\|^{2}+\epsilon\log\exp\left(\frac{4\tilde{C}_{G}^{2}\sigma^{2}}{\epsilon^{2}}\|x-\mathbb{E}(\mu)\|^{2}+\frac{1}{\epsilon}\|x-\mathbb{E}(\mu)\|\|\mathbb{E}(\nu)\|\right)

(35)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}M_{2}(\mu)-\frac{1}{2}\|x\|^{2}+\frac{4\tilde{C}_{G}^{2}\sigma^{2}}{\epsilon}\|x-\mathbb{E}(\mu)\|^{2}+\|x-\mathbb{E}(\mu)\|\|\mathbb{E}(\nu)\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}M_{2}(\mu)-\frac{1}{2}\|x\|^{2}+\frac{8\tilde{C}_{G}^{2}\sigma^{2}}{\epsilon}\|x\|^{2}+\frac{8\tilde{C}_{G}^{2}\sigma^{2}}{\epsilon}\|\mathbb{E}(\mu)\|^{2}+\|\mathbb{E}(\nu)\|\left(\|x\|+\|\mathbb{E}(\mu)\|\right)

(36)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}M_{2}(\mu)-\frac{1}{2}\|x\|^{2}+\frac{8\tilde{C}_{G}^{2}\sigma^{2}}{\epsilon}\|x\|^{2}+\frac{8\tilde{C}_{G}^{2}\sigma^{2}}{\epsilon}\|\mathbb{E}(\mu)\|^{2}+\|\mathbb{E}(\nu)\|\left(1+\|\mathbb{E}(\mu)\|\right)+\|\mathbb{E}(\nu)\|\|x\|^{2},

where in (
33
) we applied Lemma
B.9
to
g_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}^{\epsilon}
, in (
34
) we expanded
\|.\|^{2}
, in (
35
) we applied Corollary
B.8
, and in (
36
) we applied Young’s inequality and the triangle inequality. Combining various constants and multiplying both sides by
-1
, we obtain our result.
∎

We combine Lemma
B.9
and Lemma
B.10
to give a simple bound on the absolute value of
f^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}
with
\nu\in\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
:

Corollary B.11
.

Let
\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, let
\nu\in\mathcal{G}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, and choose entropic potentials that solve (
2
) such that
\int f^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}d\mu\geq 0
and
\int g^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}d\nu\geq 0
. Then there exists a constant
\tilde{C}_{\epsilon,\sigma,\|\mathbb{E}(\mu)\|,M_{2}(\mu),M_{2}(\nu)}>0
only depending quadratically on
\sigma,\|\mathbb{E}(\mu)\|
and linearly on
\frac{1}{\epsilon},\|\mathbb{E}(\nu)\|,M_{2}(\mu),M_{2}(\nu)
, such that:

|f^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}(x)|\leq\tilde{C}_{\epsilon,\sigma,\|\mathbb{E}(\mu)\|,\|\mathbb{E}(\nu)\|,M_{2}(\mu),M_{2}(\nu)}(\|x\|^{2}+1)

Proof.

From Lemma
B.9
, we have

\displaystyle f^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}\int\|x-y\|^{2}d\nu(y)

\displaystyle\leq\|x\|^{2}+\int\|y\|^{2}d\nu(y)

\displaystyle=\|x\|^{2}+M_{2}(\nu),

where we applied Young’s inequality. From this and Lemma
B.10
, we have

|f^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}(x)|\leq\max\{C_{\epsilon,\sigma,\|\mathbb{E}(\mu)\|,M_{2}(\mu),\|\mathbb{E}(\nu)\|}\left(\|x\|^{2}+1\right),\;\|x\|^{2}+M_{2}(\nu)\},

where
C_{\epsilon,\sigma,\|\mathbb{E}(\mu)\|,M_{2}(\mu)}
depends quadratically on
\sigma,\|\mathbb{E}(\mu)\|
and linearly on
\frac{1}{\epsilon},M_{2}(\mu)
and
\|\mathbb{E}(\nu)\|
. We conclude by combining constants.
∎

We now prove the differentiability of
OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu,\nu)
as a function of
\mu
. A crucial step is establishing the continuity of the entropic potentials along perturbations of
\mu
:

Proposition B.12
.

Let
\mu,\rho\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and
\nu\in\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, let
\chi:=\rho-\mu
, and let
\mu_{t}:=\mu+t\chi.
For all
t\in(0,1]
, let
f^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t}\rightarrow\nu}
be the unique entropic potential such that
\int f^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t}\rightarrow\nu}d\mu_{t}=\frac{1}{2}OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu_{t},\nu)
. Then
f_{\mu_{t}\rightarrow\nu}^{\epsilon}
converges pointwise to
f^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}
with
\int f_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}^{\epsilon}d\mu=\frac{1}{2}OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu,\nu)
as
t\rightarrow 0^{+}.

We defer its proof to Subsection
B.3
.

Proof of Theorem
2.2
:
For convenience, set
\epsilon=1
. Let
\rho\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
\chi
and
\mu_{t}
be as in the statement of Proposition
B.12
. We write
(f_{t},g_{t}):=(f^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t}\rightarrow\nu},g^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t}\rightarrow\nu})
and
(f,g):=(f^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu},g^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu})
. Let
\Delta_{t}:=\frac{1}{t}\left(OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu_{t},\nu)-OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu,\nu)\right)
. By suboptimality of
(f,g)
for the dual formulation of
OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu_{t},\nu)
and of
(f_{t},g_{t})
for
OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu,\nu)
, we have the inequalities:

\displaystyle OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu_{t},\nu)

\displaystyle\geq\int fd\mu_{t}+\int gd\nu-\int\int\left(\exp\left(f(x)+g(y)-\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}\right)-1\right)d\mu_{t}(x)d\nu(y),

(37)

\displaystyle OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu,\nu)

\displaystyle\geq\int f_{t}d\mu+\int g_{t}d\nu-\int\int\left(\exp\left(f_{t}(x)+g_{t}(y)-\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}\right)-1\right)d\mu(x)d\nu(y).

(38)

Replacing
OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu_{t},\nu)
with the lower bound in (
37
), we obtain a lower bound on
\Delta_{t}:

\displaystyle\Delta_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t}(OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu_{t},\nu)-OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu,\nu))

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{t}\bigg(\int fd\mu_{t}+\int gd\nu-\int\int\left(\exp\bigg(f(x)+g(y)-\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}\right)-1\bigg)d\mu_{t}(x)d\nu(y)

\displaystyle-\int fd\mu-\int gd\nu+\int\int\left(\exp\bigg(f(x)+g(y)-\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}\right)-1\bigg)d\mu(x)d\nu(y)\bigg)

\displaystyle=\int fd\chi-\int\int\left(\exp\bigg(f(x)+g(y)-\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}\right)-1\bigg)d\chi(x)d\nu(y).

(39)

Similarly, replacing
OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu,\nu)
with the lower bound in (
38
), we obtain:

\displaystyle\Delta_{t}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{t}(OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu_{t},\nu)-OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu,\nu))

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{t}\bigg(\int f_{t}d\mu_{t}+\int g_{t}d\nu-\int\int\left(\exp\bigg(f_{t}(x)+g_{t}(y)-\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}\right)-1\bigg)d\mu_{t}(x)d\nu(y)

\displaystyle-\int f_{t}d\mu-\int g_{t}d\nu+\int\int\left(\exp\bigg(f_{t}(x)+g_{t}(y)-\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}\right)-1\bigg)d\mu(x)d\nu(y)\bigg)

\displaystyle=\int f_{t}d\chi-\int\int\left(\exp\bigg(f_{t}(x)+g_{t}(y)-\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}\right)-1\bigg)d\chi(x)d\nu(y).

(40)

Subtracting (
39
) from (
40
), we obtain:

\displaystyle\int(f_{t}-f)d\chi

\displaystyle-\int\int\exp\left(f_{t}(x)+g_{t}(y)-\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}\right)d\chi(x)d\nu(y)

\displaystyle+\int\int\exp\left(f(x)+g(y)-\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}\right)d\chi(x)d\nu(y),

(41)

which by construction is an upper bound on
\displaystyle\limsup_{t\rightarrow 0^{+}}\Delta_{t}-\displaystyle\liminf_{t\rightarrow 0^{+}}\Delta_{t}
, and hence if we show (
B.2
) converges to 0 then we will have established existence of
\lim_{t\rightarrow 0^{+}}\Delta_{t}
. We now show that the first term converges to zero. For any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, we bound:

\displaystyle|f_{t}(x)-f(x)|\leq|f_{t}(x)|+|f(x)|\leq C_{t}^{*}(\|x\|^{2}+1),

where we applied the triangle inequality followed by Corollary
B.11
and where
C^{*}_{t}
is a constant quadratically depending on
\sigma,\|\mathbb{E}(\mu_{t})\|,\|\mathbb{E}(\mu)\|
and linearly depending on
\frac{1}{\epsilon},\|\mathbb{E}(\nu)\|,M_{2}(\mu_{t}),M_{2}(\mu),M_{2}(\nu)
. As we have
\|\mathbb{E}(\mu_{t})\|\leq\|\mathbb{E}(\mu)\|+\|\mathbb{E}(\rho)\|
and
M_{2}(\mu_{t})\leq M_{2}(\mu)+M_{2}(\rho)
, we can further upper bound
\sup_{t\in[0,1]}C_{t}^{*}\leq C^{*}
, where
C^{*}
depends only on
\frac{1}{\epsilon},\sigma,\|\mathbb{E}(\mu)\|,\|\mathbb{E}(\nu)\|,\|\mathbb{E}(\rho)\|,M_{2}(\mu),M_{2}(\rho)
, and
M_{2}(\nu)
, and is independent of
t
and
x
. Since
\mu,\rho\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
C^{*}(\|x\|^{2}+1)
is
\chi
-integrable as a function of
x
. Hence by the Dominated Convergence Theorem and Proposition
B.12
, we have that
\lim_{t\rightarrow 0^{+}}\int|f_{t}-f|d\chi=\int\lim_{t\rightarrow 0^{+}}|f_{t}-f|d\chi=0
.

Next, we claim that

\int\int\exp\left(f_{t}(x)+g_{t}(y)-\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}\right)d\chi(x)d\nu(y)=0

for all
t\in(0,1]
. Applying the Fubini-Tonelli Theorem (Proposition 5.2.1 in
[
17
]
), which applies since the integrand is nonnegative and measurable, we have, for all
t\in(0,1]
:

\displaystyle\int\int\exp\left(f_{t}(x)+g_{t}(y)-\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}\right)d\chi(x)d\nu(y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\exp(f_{t}(x))\int\exp\left(g_{t}(y)-\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}\right)d\nu(y)d\chi(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\exp\left(f_{t}(x)\right)\exp\left(-f_{t}(x)\right)d\chi(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int 1d\chi(x)=0,

(42)

where we applied (
3
) and (
4
) and the fact that
\chi=\rho-\mu
has total mass zero. By the same reasoning,

\displaystyle\int\int\exp\left(f(x)+g(y)-\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}\right)d\chi(x)d\nu(y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\exp(f(x))\int\exp\left(g(y)-\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}\right)d\nu(y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\exp(f(x))\exp(-f(x))d\chi(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int 1d\chi(x)=0.

(43)

By (
42
) and (
43
), we have established that (
B.2
) equals
\int(f_{t}-f)d\chi
for all
t\in(0,1]
, and hence converges to zero as
t\rightarrow 0^{+}
as desired. This establishes that
\lim_{t\rightarrow 0^{+}}\Delta_{t}
exists. and we compute this limit using (
39
) and (
4
) which reduces to
\int fd\chi
, by (
42
), and we conclude that
\lim_{t\rightarrow 0^{+}}\Delta_{t}=\int fd\chi
. Thus we have established that
f
is a derivative for
OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu,\nu)
as a function of
\mu
. ∎

Remark B.13
.

The above proof hinges on the fact that the Sinkhorn relation (
3
) holds for
f^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
(and the same for
f^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t}\rightarrow\nu}
). In particular, this is crucial in establishing (
43
). Without (
3
), one would need to argue that

\displaystyle\int\int

\displaystyle\exp\left(f_{t}(x)+g_{t}(y)-\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}\right)d\chi(x)d\nu(y)

\displaystyle\rightarrow\int\int

\displaystyle\exp\left(f(x)+g(y)-\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}\right)d\chi(x)d\nu(y)

as
t\rightarrow 0^{+}
directly, which is challenging as
\exp(f_{t}(x)-\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2})
is not a priori uniformly bounded over
t>0
by a
\chi
-integrable function. Hence it is not obvious whether a naive application of the Dominated Convergence Theorem will allow for the verification of this limit.

B.3
Proof of Proposition
B.12

To establish Proposition
B.12
, we will need to show that the sequence of potentials converges in probability:

Proposition B.14
.

Let
\mu,\rho,\nu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, let
\chi:=\rho-\mu
and let
\mu_{t}=\mu+t\chi
. For all
t\in[0,1)
, let
(f^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t}\rightarrow\nu},g^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t}\rightarrow\nu})
be the unique entropic potentials such that
\int f^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t}\rightarrow\nu}d\mu_{t}=\int g^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t}\rightarrow\nu}d\nu=\frac{1}{2}OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu_{t},\nu)
. Then:

•

f^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t}\rightarrow\nu}
converges in
\mu
-probability as
t\rightarrow 0^{+}
to the entropic potential
f^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}
with
\int f^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}d\mu=\frac{1}{2}OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu,\nu)
;

•

g^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t}\rightarrow\nu}
converges in
\nu
-probability as
t\rightarrow 0^{+}
to the entropic potential
g^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}
with
\int g^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}d\nu=\frac{1}{2}OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu,\nu)
.

Remark B.15
.

We note that the normalization
\int f^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t}\rightarrow\nu}d\mu_{t}=\int g^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t}\rightarrow\nu}d\nu=\frac{1}{2}OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu_{t},\nu)\geq 0
is convenient as it allows us to apply Lemma
B.9
, and also varies smoothly as a function of
t
(see e.g., Lemma
B.18
).

We postpone the proof of Proposition
B.14
to Subsection
B.4
, and continue with the proof of Proposition
B.12
.

Proof of Proposition
B.12
:
For convenience, we will assume that
\epsilon=1
. Let
f_{t}:=f^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t}\rightarrow\nu}
and
g_{t}:=g^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t}\rightarrow\nu}
for any
t\in(0,1]
and let
f:=f_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}^{\epsilon}
and
g:=g_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}^{\epsilon}
be the unique entropic potentials such that
\frac{1}{2}OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu,\nu)=\int gd\nu
. Let
T:=\{t_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\subset[0,1)
, with
t_{i}\rightarrow 0
as
i\rightarrow\infty
. By Proposition
B.14
, the sequence
\{g_{t_{i}}\}_{t_{i}\in T}
converges in
\nu
-probability to
g
. Since
\{g_{t_{i}}\}_{t_{i}\in T}
converges in probability, there is a subsequence
T^{\prime}\subset T
such that
\{g_{t_{i}^{\prime}}\}_{t_{i}^{\prime}\in T^{\prime}}
converges
\nu
-almost-surely to
g
. We now show that this, together with subgaussian assumption on
\nu
, is enough to prove pointwise convergence everywhere of
\{f_{t_{i}^{\prime}}\}_{t_{i}^{\prime}\in T^{\prime}}
to
f
. Indeed, by (
3
), for any
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
we may write:

\lim_{i\rightarrow\infty}\exp\left(-f_{t_{i}^{\prime}}(x)\right)=\lim_{i\rightarrow\infty}\int\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}+g_{t_{i}^{\prime}}(y)\right)d\nu(y).

By Lemma
B.9
, we may bound uniformly:

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}+g_{t_{i}^{\prime}}(y)\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|x\|^{2}+\langle x,y\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\|y\|^{2}+\int\frac{1}{2}\|z-y\|^{2}d\mu_{t_{i}^{\prime}}(z)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|x\|^{2}+\langle x,y\rangle-\frac{1}{2}\|y\|^{2}+\int\left(\frac{1}{2}\|z\|^{2}-\langle y,z\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\|y\|^{2}\right)d\mu_{t_{i}^{\prime}}(z)\right)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|x\|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}M_{2}(\mu_{t_{i}^{\prime}})+\langle x-\mathbb{E}(\mu_{t_{i}^{\prime}}),y\rangle\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|x\|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}M_{2}((1-t_{i}^{\prime})\mu+t_{i}^{\prime}\rho)+|\langle x,y\rangle|+|\langle\mathbb{E}((1-t_{i}^{\prime})\mu+t_{i}^{\prime}\rho),y\rangle|\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|x\|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}M_{2}(\mu)+\frac{1}{2}M_{2}(\rho)+|\langle x,y\rangle|+|\langle\mathbb{E}(\mu),y\rangle|+|\langle\mathbb{E}(\rho),y\rangle|\right)

(44)

where we applied the triangle inequality twice to obtain
|\langle x-\mathbb{E}(\mu_{t}),y\rangle|\leq|\langle x,y\rangle|+|\langle\mathbb{E}(\mu_{t}),y\rangle|
and
|\langle\mathbb{E}(\mu_{t}),y\rangle|\leq|\langle\mathbb{E}(\mu),y\rangle|+|\langle\mathbb{E}(\rho),y\rangle|
, and also used that
0<t_{i}^{\prime}\leq 1
. We now show that the function in (
44
) is
\nu
-integrable in
y
. By two applications of the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, we bound:

\displaystyle\int\exp\left(|\langle x,y\rangle|+|\langle\mathbb{E}(\mu),y\rangle|+|\langle\mathbb{E}(\rho),y\rangle|\right)d\nu(y)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int\exp\left(|\langle x,y\rangle|\right)\exp\left(|\langle\mathbb{E}(\mu),y\rangle|\right)\exp\left(|\langle\mathbb{E}(\rho),y\rangle|\right)d\nu(y)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\int\exp\left(2|\langle x,y\rangle|\right)d\nu(y)\right)^{1/2}\left(\int\exp\left(4|\langle\mathbb{E}(\mu),y\rangle|\right)d\nu(y)\int\exp\left(4|\langle\mathbb{E}(\rho),y\rangle|\right)d\nu(y)\right)^{1/4}

and by Corollary
B.8
this last expression is finite. Hence (
44
) is
\nu
-integrable, and we conclude that for each
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}+g_{t_{i}^{\prime}}(y)\right)
may be uniformly (in
t_{i}^{\prime}
) upper bounded by a
\nu
-integrable function of
y
. By the Dominated Convergence Theorem, we have for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
:

\displaystyle\lim_{i\rightarrow\infty}\exp\left(-f_{t_{i}^{\prime}}(x)\right)

\displaystyle=\int\lim_{i\rightarrow\infty}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}+g_{t_{i}^{\prime}}(y)\right)d\nu(y)

\displaystyle=\int\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}+g(y)\right)d\nu(y)

\displaystyle=\exp(-f(x))

by
\nu
-almost sure convergence of
g_{t_{i}^{\prime}}
to
g
, which, along with continuity of
\log
, establishes pointwise everywhere convergence of
\{f_{t_{i}^{\prime}}\}_{t_{i}^{\prime}\in T^{\prime}}.

We claim this is enough to conclude pointwise everywhere convergence of
\{f_{t_{i}}\}_{t_{i}\in T}
to
f
. Indeed, suppose
\{f_{t_{i}}\}_{t_{i}\in T}
does not converge pointwise everywhere to
f
. Then there exists an
x_{0}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, a
\delta>0
, and a subsequence
\tilde{T}\subset T
with
\tilde{t}_{i}\rightarrow 0
as
i\rightarrow\infty
, such that
\|f(x_{0})-f_{\tilde{t}_{i}}(x_{0})\|>\delta
for all
\tilde{t}_{i}\in\tilde{T}
. But
\{g_{\tilde{t}_{i}}\}_{\tilde{t}_{i}\in\tilde{T}}
converges in
\nu
-probability to
g
, and thus the sequence
\tilde{T}
contains a subsequence
\tilde{T}^{\prime}\subset\tilde{T}
along which
\{g_{\tilde{t}_{i}^{\prime}}\}_{\tilde{t}_{i}^{\prime}\in\tilde{T}^{\prime}}
converges
\nu
-almost surely to
g
. Hence we may apply the above argument to conclude that
f_{\tilde{t}_{i}^{\prime}}
converges pointwise to
f
, contradicting our assumption. We conclude that
\{f_{t_{i}}\}_{t_{i}\in T}
converges pointwise to
f
as
i\rightarrow\infty
.
∎

B.4
Proof of Proposition
B.14

To prove Proposition
B.14
, we will first need to verify that our sequence
\mu_{t}
contains a subsequence satisfying the conditions in the lemma below:

Lemma B.16
.

(Corollary 2.4 in
[
45
]
) Let
T:=\{t_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\subset[0,1)
be any sequence such that
t_{i}\rightarrow 0^{+}
as
i\rightarrow\infty.
Let
\{\mu_{t_{i}}\}_{t_{i}\in T}\subset\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
be a sequence of probability measures such that:

1.

\mu_{t_{i}}
converges weakly to
\mu
as
i\rightarrow\infty
;

2.

\mu\ll\mu_{t_{i}}
for all
t_{i}\in[0,1)
;

3.

OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu_{t_{i}},\nu)<\infty
for all
t_{i}\in[0,1]
;

4.

\sup_{i}\int\max\{f_{\mu_{t_{i}}\rightarrow\nu}^{\epsilon},0\}d\mu_{t_{i}}<\infty
,
\sup_{i}\int\max\{g_{\mu_{t_{i}}\rightarrow\nu}^{\epsilon},0\}d\nu<\infty
;

5.

\displaystyle\lim_{i\rightarrow\infty}\int\arctan(f^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t_{i}}\rightarrow\nu})d\mu_{t_{i}}=L
for some
L\in[-\pi/2,\pi/2]
;

6.

\displaystyle\limsup_{C\rightarrow\infty}\limsup_{i\rightarrow\infty}\int\mathbbm{1}_{\frac{d\mu}{d\mu_{t_{i}}}(x)\geq C}d\mu(x)=0.

Then:

•

f_{\mu_{t_{i}}\rightarrow\nu}^{\epsilon}
converges in
\mu
-probability to
f^{\epsilon,L}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}
, the unique entropic potential with
\int\arctan(f^{\epsilon,L}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu})d\mu=L
;

•

g^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t_{i}}\rightarrow\nu}
converges in
\nu
-probability to
g^{\epsilon,L}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}
, such that
g^{\epsilon,L}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}
is the unique entropic potential paired with
f^{\epsilon,L}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}
.

We remark that, for any
f\in L^{1}(\mu)
,
\int\arctan(f+a)d\mu
is a strictly increasing function of
a\in\mathbb{R}
. Hence if there are two entropic potentials
f^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu},\tilde{f}^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}
such that:

•

f^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}+c=\tilde{f}^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}
for some
c\in\mathbb{R}
;

•

\int\arctan(f^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu})d\mu=\int\arctan(\tilde{f}^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu})d\mu
;

then
c=0
, and consequently
f^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}=\tilde{f}^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}
. This justifies the uniqueness claimed for
f^{\epsilon,L}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}
(and hence for
g^{\epsilon,L}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}
) in Lemma
B.16
.

To verify item 6 we will apply the following Lemma:

Lemma B.17
.

(Lemma 2.5 in
[
45
]
) Let
T:=\{t_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\subset[0,1)
be any sequence such that
t_{i}\rightarrow 0^{+}
as
i\rightarrow\infty
, and let
\{\mu_{t_{i}}\}_{t_{i}\in T}\subset\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
be a sequence of probability measures such that
\mu\ll\mu_{t_{i}}
for all
t_{i}\in T
. Suppose that
\mu_{t_{i}}\rightarrow\mu
in total variation as
i\rightarrow\infty
. Then:

\limsup_{C\rightarrow\infty}\limsup_{i\rightarrow\infty}\int\mathbbm{1}_{\frac{d\mu}{d\mu_{t_{i}}}(x)\geq C}d\mu(x)=0.

After verifying the conditions in Lemma
B.16
, we will need to establish that the limiting potentials satisfy
\int f^{\epsilon,L}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}d\mu=\frac{1}{2}OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu,\nu)
,
\int g^{\epsilon,L}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}d\mu=\frac{1}{2}OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu,\nu)
. The result immediately follows from the Lemma below under the stated assumption in Proposition
B.14
that
\int f_{t}d\mu_{t}=\int g_{t}d\nu=\frac{1}{2}OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu_{t},\nu)
.

Lemma B.18
.

(Theorem 2.1 (iii) in
[
45
]
) Let
T:=\{t_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\subset[0,1)
be any sequence such that
t_{i}\rightarrow 0^{+}
as
i\rightarrow\infty
, and let
\{\mu_{t_{i}}\}_{t_{i}\in T}\subset\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
be a sequence of probability measures, converging weakly to
\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, and let
\nu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d}).

Let
(f_{t_{i}},g_{t_{i}}):=(f^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t_{i}}\rightarrow\nu},g^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t_{i}}\rightarrow\nu})
be solutions to (
2
), and suppose that:

1.

\sup_{i}\{\int f_{t_{i}}^{\epsilon}d\mu_{t_{i}},\int g_{t_{i}}^{\epsilon}d\mu_{t_{i}}\}<\infty
.

2.

\{(f_{t_{i}},g_{t_{i}})\}_{t_{i}\in T}
are uniformly integrable with respect to
(\mu_{t_{i}},\nu)
:

\lim_{C\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\int f_{t_{i}}(x)\mathbbm{1}_{f_{t_{i}}>C}(x)d\mu_{t_{i}}(x)=0,

\lim_{C\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\int g_{t_{i}}(y)\mathbbm{1}_{g_{t_{i}}>C}(y)d\nu(y)=0.

3.

\displaystyle\lim_{i\rightarrow\infty}\int\arctan(f_{t_{i}})d\mu_{t_{i}}=\int\arctan(f)d\mu
.

Then:

•

OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu_{t_{i}},\nu)\rightarrow OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu,\nu)
as
i\rightarrow\infty.

•

\int f_{t_{i}}d\mu_{t_{i}}\rightarrow\int fd\mu
as
i\rightarrow\infty
.

•

\int g_{t_{i}}d\nu\rightarrow\int gd\nu
as
i\rightarrow\infty.

To verify item 2 in Lemma
B.18
, we will require the following result:

Lemma B.19
.

Let
\mu,\rho,\nu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. We let
\chi=\rho-\mu
, and define a sequence of probability measures
\mu_{t_{i}}:=\mu+t_{i}\chi
where
\{t_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}=:T\subset[0,1]
is a sequence of nonnegative real numbers such that
t_{i}\rightarrow 0^{+}
as
i\rightarrow\infty
. Then the sequence of potentials
\{(f^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t_{i}}\rightarrow\nu},g^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t_{i}}\rightarrow\nu})\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
satisfies:

\lim_{C\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\int f_{\mu_{t_{i}}\rightarrow\nu}^{\epsilon}(x)\mathbbm{1}_{f^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t_{i}}\rightarrow\nu}>C}(x)d\mu_{t_{i}}(x)=0,

(45)

\lim_{C\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\int g_{\mu_{t_{i}}\rightarrow\nu}^{\epsilon}(y)\mathbbm{1}_{g^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t_{i}}\rightarrow\nu}>C}(y)d\nu(y)=0.

(46)

Proof.

Without loss of generality, let
\epsilon=1
. We bound:

\displaystyle\int f_{\mu_{t_{i}}\rightarrow\nu}^{\epsilon}(x)\mathbbm{1}_{f^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t_{i}}\rightarrow\nu}>C}(x)d\mu_{t_{i}}(x)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int\left(\int\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}d\nu(y)\right)\mathbbm{1}_{f^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t_{i}}\rightarrow\nu}>C}(x)d\mu_{t_{i}}(x)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int\left(\int\|x\|^{2}+\|y\|^{2}d\nu(y)\right)\mathbbm{1}_{f^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t_{i}}\rightarrow\nu}>C}(x)d\mu_{t_{i}}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int(\|x\|^{2}+M_{2}(\nu))\mathbbm{1}_{f^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t_{i}}\rightarrow\nu}>C}(x)d\mu_{t_{i}}(x)

where we applied Lemma
B.9
, the triangle inequality, and Young’s inequality. We also note that:

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}_{f^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t_{i}}\rightarrow\nu}>C}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbbm{1}_{\frac{1}{2}\int\|x-y\|^{2}d\nu(y)>C}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbbm{1}_{\|.\|^{2}+M_{2}(\nu)>C}(x)

\displaystyle=\mathbbm{1}_{\|.\|^{2}>C-M_{2}(\nu)}(x)

where we applied Lemma
B.9
, the triangle inequality, and Young’s inequality. As
2\|x\|^{2}\geq\|x\|^{2}+M_{2}(\nu)
for all
\|x\|^{2}\geq M_{2}(\nu)
we have:

\displaystyle\lim_{C\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\int\left(\|x\|^{2}+M_{2}(\nu)\right)\mathbbm{1}_{\|.\|^{2}>C-M_{2}(\nu)}(x)d\mu_{t_{i}}(x)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\lim_{D\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}2\int\|x\|^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{\|.\|^{2}>D}(x)d\mu_{t_{i}}(x)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\lim_{D\rightarrow\infty}\sup_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\left((1-t_{i})\int||x||^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{||.||^{2}>D}(x)d\mu(x)+t_{i}\int||x||^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{||.||^{2}>D}(x)d\rho(x)\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\lim_{D\rightarrow\infty}\left(\int||x||^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{||.||^{2}>D}(x)d\mu(x)+\int||x||^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{||.||^{2}>D}(x)d\rho(x)\right)

(47)

Since
\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,

\displaystyle M_{2}(\mu)=\int||x||^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{||.||^{2}>D}(x)d\mu+\int||x||^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{||.||^{2}\leq D}(x)d\mu<\infty,

and by the monotone convergence theorem
\lim_{D\rightarrow\infty}\int||x||^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{||.||^{2}\leq D}(x)d\mu=M_{2}(\mu)
, and hence

\limsup_{D\rightarrow\infty}\int||x||^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{||.||^{2}>D}(x)d\mu=0
. Similarly, since
\rho\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
\lim_{D\rightarrow\infty}\int||x||^{2}\mathbbm{1}_{||.||^{2}>D}(x)d\rho=0
, and (
47
) is zero.

The proof for
g^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t_{i}}\rightarrow\nu}
follows similarly.
∎

Proof of Proposition
B.14
:
For convenience, we will assume that
\epsilon=1
. Let
T:=\{t_{i}\}_{i\in\mathbb{N}}\subset[0,1)
be any sequence such that
t_{i}\rightarrow 0^{+}
as
i\rightarrow\infty.
We begin by verifying that the conditions in Lemma
B.16
hold along a subsequence
T^{\prime}\subset T
. Items 1 and 2 clearly hold for any subsequence of
T
by definition of
\mu_{t_{i}}=(1-t_{i})\mu+t_{i}\rho
.

We may establish item 4 for any subsequence of
T
via Lemma
B.9
and Young’s inequality:

\displaystyle\int\max\{f_{\mu_{t_{i}}\rightarrow\nu}^{\epsilon},0\}d\mu_{t_{i}}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\int\int\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}d\nu(y)d\mu_{t_{i}}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\int(\|x\|^{2}+M_{2}(\nu))d\mu_{t_{i}}(x)

\displaystyle=M_{2}(\mu_{t_{i}})+M_{2}(\nu)

\displaystyle\leq M_{2}(\mu)+M_{2}(\rho)+M_{2}(\nu)

and hence
\sup_{i}\int\max\{f_{\mu_{t_{i}}\rightarrow\nu}^{\epsilon}(x),0\}d\mu_{t_{i}}(x)<\infty
, and the same holds for
\sup_{i}\int\max\{g_{\mu_{t_{i}}\rightarrow\nu}(y),0\}d\nu(y)
. This also verifies item 3, since
\int f^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t_{i}}\rightarrow\nu}d\mu_{t_{i}}+\int g^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t_{i}}\rightarrow\nu}d\nu=OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu_{t_{i}},\nu)
.

To see item 5, since
\arctan
is bounded,
\int\arctan(f^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t_{i}}\rightarrow\nu})d\mu_{t_{i}}
is uniformly bounded, and hence there exists a convergent subsequence
T^{\prime}
with
\displaystyle\lim_{i\rightarrow\infty}\int\arctan(f^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t_{i}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\nu})d\mu_{t_{i}^{\prime}}=L
for some
L\in[-\pi/2,\pi/2]
. To verify item 6, the sequence
\mu_{t_{i}}
clearly converges to
\mu
in total variation
2
2

2

The
total variation
distance between
\mu
and
\nu
is

d_{TV}(\mu,\nu)=\sup_{A}|\mu(A)-\nu(A)|
where the supremum is over all measurable sets
A
.
, and hence we may apply Lemma
B.17
to conclude that:

\limsup_{C\rightarrow\infty}\limsup_{i\rightarrow\infty}\int\mathbbm{1}_{\frac{d\mu}{d\mu_{t_{i}}}(x)\geq C}d\mu(x)=0.

We have thus established that Lemma
B.16
holds for the subsequence indexed by
T^{\prime}
, we conclude that
\{f^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t_{i}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\nu}\}_{t_{i}^{\prime}\in T^{\prime}}
converges in
\mu
-probability to
f^{\epsilon,L}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}
, and similarly
\{g^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t_{i}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\nu}\}_{t_{i}^{\prime}\in T^{\prime}}
converges in
\nu
-probability to
g^{\epsilon,L}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}.

Next, we verify the conditions in Lemma
B.18
for the subsequence
(f_{\mu_{t_{i}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\nu}^{\epsilon},g_{\mu_{t_{i}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\nu}^{\epsilon})
. Indeed, item 1 has already been established in the verification of the conditions of
B.16
above, and by definition of the subsequence, item 3 holds with limit potentials
(f^{\epsilon,L}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu},g^{\epsilon,L}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}
). By Lemma
B.19
, this subsequence also satisfies (
45
) and (
46
), establishing item 2. Hence we have verified Lemma
B.18
for our subsequence, and can conclude that
\int f^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t_{i}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\nu}d\mu_{t_{i}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\int f^{\epsilon,L}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}d\mu
and
\int g^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t_{i}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\nu}d\nu\rightarrow\int g^{\epsilon,L}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}d\nu.
By assumption, we have that
\int f^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t_{i}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\nu}d\mu_{t_{i}^{\prime}}=\int g^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{t_{i}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\nu}d\nu=\frac{1}{2}OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu_{t_{i}^{\prime}},\nu)
, and again by Lemma
B.18
we have
\lim_{i\rightarrow\infty}OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu_{t_{i}^{\prime}},\nu)=OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu,\nu)
. We conclude that

\int f^{\epsilon,L}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}d\mu=\int g^{\epsilon,L}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}d\nu=\frac{1}{2}OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu,\nu).

(48)

Hence we have identified
(f^{\epsilon,L}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu},g^{\epsilon,L}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu})=(f^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu},g^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu})
, the unique pair of potentials satisfying (
48
).

Since our sequence
T
was arbitrary, we can conclude as follows. Let
T=\{t_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
be a sequence with
t_{i}\rightarrow 0^{+}
as
i\rightarrow\infty
, and suppose that
f_{\mu_{t_{i}}\rightarrow\nu}
does not converge in
\mu
-probability to
f_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}^{\epsilon}
. Since convergence in probability is metrizable, there exists a metric
d
and a
\kappa>0
such that there exists a subsequence
S
of
T
with the property that
d(f_{\mu_{s_{i}}\rightarrow\nu}^{\epsilon},f^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu})>\kappa
for all
s_{i}\in S
. The above proof also applies to
S
, and hence we may find a subsequence
S^{\prime}\subset S
so that
f_{\mu_{s_{i}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\nu}
converges to
f_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}^{\epsilon}
in
\mu
-probability, and hence
d(f_{\mu_{s_{i}^{\prime}}\rightarrow\nu}^{\epsilon},f^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu})\rightarrow 0
as
i\rightarrow\infty
which is a contradiction. Thus we may conclude that
f_{\mu_{t_{i}}\rightarrow\nu}^{\epsilon}
converges to
f_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}^{\epsilon}
in
\mu
-probability.
The same argument allow us to conclude for
g_{\mu_{t_{i}}\rightarrow\nu}^{\epsilon}.

∎

B.5
Proof of Proposition
2.3

For convenience, we set
\epsilon=1
. Recall that we may write

f^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}(x)=-\log\left(\int\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}+g^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}(y)\right)d\nu(y)\right).

We define
h(x,y):=\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}+g^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}(y)\right)
, and will show that
\nabla_{x}\displaystyle\int h(x,y)d\nu(y)
exists. Define

h_{t,i}(x,y):=\frac{1}{t}\left(\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|x+te_{i}-y\|^{2}+g^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}(y)\right)-\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}+g^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}(y)\right)\right)

where
e_{i}
is a standard basis vector in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
. We then define
h_{t}=(h_{t,1},...,h_{t,d})
, and observe that:

\displaystyle\lim_{t\rightarrow 0^{+}}h_{t}(x,y)

\displaystyle=\nabla_{x}h(x,y)

\displaystyle=-(x-y)\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}+g^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}(y)\right).

At each
x
, we apply the mean value theorem to write
h_{t}(x,y)
as
\|\nabla_{x}h(\theta_{x}^{t},y)\|
for some
\theta^{t}_{x}\in B_{t}(x):=\{y\in\mathbb{R}^{d}\ |\ \|x-y\|<t\}
. We upper bound:

\displaystyle|h_{t}(x,y)|

\displaystyle=\|\nabla_{x}h(\theta^{t}_{x},y)\|

\displaystyle=\left\|(\theta^{t}_{x}-y)\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|\theta^{t}_{x}-y\|^{2}+g^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}(y)\right)\right\|

\displaystyle\leq\|\theta^{t}_{x}-y\|\;\left|\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|\theta^{t}_{x}-y\|^{2}+\int\frac{1}{2}\|z-y\|^{2}d\mu(z)\right)\right|

(49)

\displaystyle=\|\theta^{t}_{x}-y\|\;\left|\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|\theta^{t}_{x}\|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}M_{2}(\mu)+\langle\theta^{t}_{x}-\mathbb{E}(\mu),y\rangle\right)\right|

where in (
49
) we applied the bound on
g^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}(y)
from Lemma
B.9
. Since
\theta^{t}_{x}\in B_{t}(x)
, we may assume WLOG that
\|\theta_{x}^{t}-x\|\leq 1
for all small enough
t\leq 1
. Hence we may uniformly (in
y
and
t
) bound:

\displaystyle|h_{t}(x,y)|

\displaystyle\leq\sup_{z\in\overline{B_{1}}(x)}\|z-y\|\;\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|z\|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}M_{2}(\mu)+\langle z,y\rangle-\langle\mathbb{E}(\mu),y\rangle\right).

We may then bound:

\displaystyle\int|h_{t}(x,y)|d\nu(y)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\int\sup_{z\in\overline{B_{1}}(x)}\|z-y\|\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|z\|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}M_{2}(\mu)+\langle z-\mathbb{E}(\mu),y\rangle\right)d\nu(y)

(50)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\int\sup_{z\in\overline{B_{1}}(x)}\|z-y\|^{2}\exp\left(-\|z\|^{2}+M_{2}(\mu)\right)d\nu(y)\right)^{1/2}\left(\int\sup_{z\in\overline{B_{1}}(x)}\exp(2\langle z-\mathbb{E}(\mu),y\rangle)d\nu(y)\right)^{1/2}

(51)

\displaystyle<

\displaystyle\infty,

where we applied the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality in (
50
) and Corollary
B.8
to conclude that (
51
) is finite. Thus for each
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
and all
t\in[0,1)
,
h_{t}(x,y)
is uniformly bounded by an integrable function of
y
in a neighborhood of
x
, and hence we may apply the Dominated Convergence Theorem to conclude that
\displaystyle\lim_{t\rightarrow 0^{+}}\int h_{t}(x,y)d\nu(y)=\int\lim_{t\rightarrow 0^{+}}h_{t}(x,y)d\nu(y)
.
Equivalently, we have shown:

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}\int\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}+g^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}(y)\right)d\nu(y)

\displaystyle=\int\nabla_{x}\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}+g^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}(y)\right)d\nu(y)

\displaystyle=\int(y-x)\exp\left(-\frac{1}{2}\|x-y\|^{2}+g^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}(y)\right)d\nu(y)

for all
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
. Since
f^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}(x)=-\displaystyle\log\left(\int h(x,y)d\nu(y)\right)
,
f^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}(x)
is a composition of two differentiable functions and is hence differentiable by the chain rule. One then easily verifies (
8
). The proof that the higher derivatives of
f^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}
exist follows similarly.

∎

B.6
Proof of Corollary
2.4

Proof.

The result immediately follows from Theorem
2.2
followed by Proposition
2.3
and linearity of derivatives.
∎

B.7
Proof of Corollary
2.5

Let
\mu^{\epsilon}
be a critical point for
F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}
with
\texttt{supp}(\mu^{\epsilon})=\Omega
. By Theorem
2.2
,
\delta F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}
is smooth, and hence
\nabla\delta F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}[x]=0
for
\mu^{\epsilon}
-almost every
x
implies that
\nabla\delta F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}[x]=0
for all
x\in\Omega
. Since
\Omega
is connected, this implies that there exists a constant
c
such that
\delta F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(x)=c
for all
x\in\Omega
. Thus
\langle\delta F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu^{\epsilon}),\mu-\mu^{\epsilon}\rangle=0
for any
\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\Omega)
satisfying the optimality criteria in Proposition
A.9
. The same proof applies to
S^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}
. ∎

B.8
Proof of Lemma
2.6

For each
1\leq j\leq m
there is a unique coupling between
\delta_{x_{\mathcal{V}}}
and
\nu_{j}
, given by
\delta_{x_{\mathcal{V}}}\otimes\nu_{j}
. We then compute the entropic map
T^{\epsilon}_{\delta_{x_{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(x_{\mathcal{V}})=\mathbb{E}_{(X,Y)\sim\delta_{x_{\mathcal{V}}}\otimes\nu_{j}}[Y|X=x_{\mathcal{V}}]=\mathbb{E}(\nu_{j})
. This holds for each
j
, and hence we may compute
\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}T^{\epsilon}_{\delta_{x_{\mathcal{V}}}\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(x_{\mathcal{V}})=x_{\mathcal{V}}
.

Hence
\delta_{x_{\mathcal{V}}}
satisfies the fixed point condition in Corollary
2.4
, and thus is a critical point.
∎

B.9
Proof of Proposition
2.7

The proof is essentially an immediate consequence of Proposition
B.23
and Lemma
B.21
stated below. In order to state the results, we first recall the following definition of convex ordering and a useful lemma.

Definition B.20
.

Two probability measures
\mu,\nu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
are in
convex order
, denoted
\mu\leq_{c}\nu
, if
\displaystyle\int\phi d\mu\leq\displaystyle\int\phi d\nu
for all convex
\phi:\mathbb{R}^{d}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
.

Lemma B.21
.

Let
\nu
be
\sigma
-subgaussian and
\mu\leq_{c}\nu
. Then
\mu
is
\sigma
-subgaussian.

Proof.

Let
X\sim\mu
and
Y\sim\nu
, and let
v\in S^{d-1}
. Then we define the function
f_{v}(x):=\exp\left(\frac{|\langle x,v\rangle|^{2}}{\sigma^{2}}\right)
, which is convex in
x
. Since
\mu\leq_{c}\nu
, we have
\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\mu}[f_{v}(X)]\leq\mathbb{E}_{Y\sim\nu}[f_{v}(Y)]
for any
v
, and since
\nu
is subgaussian, we have that
\sup_{v\in S^{d-1}}\mathbb{E}_{Y\sim\nu}[f_{v}(Y)]\leq 2
. Hence we may conclude that
\sup_{v\in S^{d-1}}\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\mu}[f_{v}(X)]\leq 2
, so that
\mu
is
\sigma
-subgaussian.
∎

We note the following result from
[
68
]
.

Lemma B.22
.

(Lemma 9.2 in
[
68
]
) Let
\mu,\nu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then
[T_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}^{\epsilon}]_{\#}(\mu)\leq_{c}\nu
.

We apply the above lemma to obtain the following result for critical points of
F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}
:

Proposition B.23
.

Suppose
\{\nu_{1},...,\nu_{m}\}=\mathcal{V}\subset\mathcal{G}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d}),
and let
\mu^{\epsilon}
be a critical point for
F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}
. Then
\mu^{\epsilon}\leq_{c}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\nu_{j}
.

Proof.

Let
\mu^{\epsilon}
be a critical point of
F_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}^{\epsilon}.
By Lemma
B.22
,
\tilde{\mu}_{j}:=[T^{\epsilon}_{\mu^{\epsilon}\rightarrow\nu_{j}}]_{\#}(\mu^{\epsilon})\leq_{c}\nu_{j}
for all
1\leq j\leq m
. As
\mu^{\epsilon}
is a critical point, we have:
[\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}T^{\epsilon}_{\mu^{\epsilon}\rightarrow\nu_{j}}]_{\#}(\mu^{\epsilon})=\mu^{\epsilon}
by Corollary
2.4
. Therefore, for any convex function
\phi(x)
:

\displaystyle\int\phi(x)d\mu^{\epsilon}(x)

\displaystyle=\int\phi(x)d\left[\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}T^{\epsilon}_{\mu^{\epsilon}\rightarrow\nu_{j}}\right]_{\#}(\mu^{\epsilon})(x)

\displaystyle=\int\phi\left(\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}T^{\epsilon}_{\mu^{\epsilon}\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(x)\right)d\mu^{\epsilon}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\int\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\phi(T_{\mu^{\epsilon}\rightarrow\nu_{j}}^{\epsilon}(x))d\mu^{\epsilon}(x)

(52)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\int\phi(x)d[T_{\mu^{\epsilon}\rightarrow\nu_{j}}^{\epsilon}]_{\#}(\mu^{\epsilon})(x)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\int\phi(x)d\tilde{\mu}_{j}(x)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\int\phi(x)d\nu_{j}(x)

(53)

\displaystyle=\int\phi(x)d\left[\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\nu_{j}\right](x),

where we applied Jensen’s inequality in (
52
) and Lemma
B.22
to assert that
\tilde{\mu}_{j}\leq_{c}\nu_{j}
in (
53
). Thus
\mu^{\epsilon}\leq_{c}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\nu_{j}
.

∎

We conclude the proof of Proposition
2.7
by observing that

\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\nu_{j}\right\|_{\mathcal{G}}\leq\max_{1\leq j\leq m}\|\nu_{j}\|_{\mathcal{G}}\leq\sigma.

∎

Appendix C
Proof of Theorem
3.1

The proof of Theorem 3.1 rests on the following propositions.

Proposition C.1
.

Let
\mathcal{V}=\{\nu_{1},...,\nu_{m}\}\subset\mathcal{G}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and let
\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n}=\{\hat{\nu}^{n}_{1},...,\hat{\nu}^{n}_{m}\}.
Then:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}F_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)-\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}F_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)\bigg|\right]\leq\frac{m^{1/2}R^{*}_{d,\epsilon,\sigma}}{\sqrt{n}},

(54)

\displaystyle\bigg|\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}F_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)-\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}^{n}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}F_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)\bigg|\leq\frac{R^{*}_{d,\epsilon,\sigma}}{\sqrt{n}},

(55)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}F_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)-\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}^{n_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}F_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n_{2}}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)\bigg|\right]\leq mR^{*}_{d,\epsilon,\sigma}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{1}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\right),

(56)

where
R^{*}_{d,\epsilon,\sigma}
is a constant depending only on
d,\epsilon
and
\sigma
.

We note the different dependencies on
m
appearing in (
54
), (
55
) and (
56
), which is possibly due to our method of proof. We leave the question of whether the dependence on
m
can be improved to future work. We give an analogous proposition for the Sinkhorn barycenter functional.

Proposition C.2
.

Suppose that, for any
\mathcal{V}\subset\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
,
U_{n}:=\argmin_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}^{n}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}S^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu)
is nonempty and furthermore
U_{n}\subset\mathcal{G}_{\tilde{\sigma}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, where
\tilde{\sigma}=\max_{1\leq j\leq m}\{\|\nu_{j}\|_{\mathcal{G}}\}
. Let
\mathcal{V}=\{\nu_{1},...,\nu_{m}\}\subset\mathcal{G}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and let
\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n}=\{\hat{\nu}_{1}^{n},...,\hat{\nu}_{m}^{n}\}
. Then:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{G}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}S_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)-\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{G}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}S_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)\bigg|\right]\leq\frac{m^{1/2}R^{*}_{d,\epsilon,\sigma}}{\sqrt{n}},

(57)

\displaystyle\bigg|\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{G}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}S_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)-\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}^{n}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}S_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)\bigg|\leq\frac{R^{*}_{d,\epsilon,\sigma}}{\sqrt{n}},

(58)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{G}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}S_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)-\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}^{n_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}S_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n_{2}}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)\bigg|\right]\leq mR^{*}_{d,\epsilon,\sigma}\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{1}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\right),

(59)

We remark that the additional assumptions present in Proposition
C.2
are to guarantee that the minimizer of the Sinkhorn barycenter functional has subgaussian constant bounded above by the maximal subgaussian constant over the reference measures. This is not needed in Proposition
C.1
, as this is implied by Proposition
B.23
. The proofs of Propositions
C.1
and Proposition
C.2
rely on three technical results established in
[
39
]
.

Proposition C.3
.

(Proposition 2 in
[
39
]
) Let
\mu,\nu
and
\hat{\nu}^{n}
be
\sigma
-subgaussian measures, where
\sigma\in[0,\infty)
is (possibly) random. Then:

|OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu,\hat{\nu}^{n})-OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu,\nu)|\leq 2\sup_{u\in\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon,\sigma}}\bigg|\int ud\nu-\int ud\hat{\nu}^{n}\bigg|,

almost-surely, where
\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon,\sigma}
is defined as the set of functions
u
such that, for any multi-index
\alpha
with
|\alpha|=k
:

|D^{\alpha}(u-\frac{1}{2}\|.\|^{2})(x)|\leq C_{\epsilon,k,d}\begin{cases}1+\sigma^{4}&\text{if}\;k=0\\
\sigma^{k}(\sigma+\sigma^{2})^{k}&\text{otherwise}\\
\end{cases}

if
\|x\|\leq\sqrt{d}\sigma
, and

|D^{\alpha}(u-\frac{1}{2}\|.\|^{2})(x)|\leq C_{\epsilon,k,d}\begin{cases}1+(1+\sigma^{2})\|x\|^{2}&\text{if}\;k=0\\
\sigma^{k}(\sqrt{\sigma\|x\|}+\sigma\|x\|)^{k}&\text{otherwise}\\
\end{cases}

if
\|x\|>\sqrt{d}\sigma
, where
C_{\epsilon,k,d}
is a constant depending only on
\epsilon,k,d
.

Proposition C.4
.

(Proof of Theorem 2,
[
39
]
)
Let
\nu
be
\sigma
-subgaussian and let
\tilde{\sigma}
be a random variable such that both
\nu
and
\hat{\nu}^{n}
are
\tilde{\sigma}
-subgaussian. Then:

\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\nu}^{n},\tilde{\sigma}}\left[\sup_{u\in\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon,\tilde{\sigma}}}\int ud\nu-\int ud\hat{\nu}^{n}\right]\leq\frac{C_{d,\epsilon}}{\sqrt{n}}(\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\sigma}}[(1+\tilde{\sigma}^{3s})^{2}])^{1/2}(1+\sigma^{d+2}),

(60)

where
s=\lceil\frac{d}{2}\rceil+1
and
C_{d,\epsilon}
is a constant depending on
d,\epsilon
.

As Proposition
C.4
is not stated exactly as a result in
[
39
]
, we give a sketch of the proof:

Proof.

For any
q\geq 2
, let
\mathcal{F}^{\epsilon,q}
to be the set of all functions with
|f(x)|\leq C_{d,\epsilon}(1+\|x\|^{2})
and
|D^{\alpha}f(x)|\leq C_{d,\epsilon}(1+\|x\|^{q})
for
|\alpha|\leq q
, where
C_{d,\epsilon}
is an absolute constant that can depend on
d
and
\epsilon
. Then it can be shown that if
u\in\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon,\tilde{\sigma}}
(the function class from Proposition
C.3
) then
\frac{u}{1+\tilde{\sigma}^{3s}}\in\mathcal{F}^{\epsilon,s}
, almost-surely. This implies the left hand side of (
60
) can be upper bound by

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\sigma},\hat{\mathcal{\nu}}^{n}}\left[(1+\tilde{\sigma}^{3s})\sup_{u\in\mathcal{F}^{\epsilon,s}}\left|\int ud\nu-\int ud\hat{\nu}^{n}\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left(\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\sigma}}[(1+\tilde{\sigma}^{3s})^{2}]\right)^{1/2}\left(\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\nu}^{n}}\left[\sup_{u\in\mathcal{F}^{\epsilon,s}}\left|\int ud\nu-\int ud\hat{\nu}^{n}\right|\right]^{2}\right)^{1/2},

where we applied the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality. Following the empirical process theory argument as in the proof of Theorem 2 in
[
39
]
, one is then able to bound

\mathbb{E}_{\hat{\nu}^{n}}\left(\left[\sup_{u\in\mathcal{F}^{\epsilon,s}}\left|\int ud\nu-\int ud\hat{\nu}^{n}\right|\right]^{2}\right)

by
\frac{C_{d,\epsilon}}{n}(1+\sigma^{2d+4})
. We conclude by applying the bound
(1+\sigma^{2d+4})^{1/2}\leq 1+\sigma^{d+2}
, which holds since
\sigma\geq 0
.
∎

Lemma C.5
.

(Lemma A.4 in
[
39
]
) Let
\nu
be
\sigma
-subgaussian. Then for all integers
k\geq 1
,

\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{\nu}^{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}}^{2k}]\leq L_{k}\sigma^{2k},

\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{\nu}^{n}\|^{2k-1}_{\mathcal{G}}]\leq\max\{L_{k}\sigma^{2k},1\}

where
L_{k}
is a constant depending on
k
.

We remark that Lemma A.4 in
[
39
]
establishes
\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{\nu}^{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}}^{2k}]\leq L_{k}\sigma^{2k}
. To extend this to odd powers, note that
\mathbb{E}[\|X\|^{k}]\leq\mathbb{E}[\|X\|^{k+1}]^{\frac{k}{k+1}}
for any random variable
X
by Jensen’s inequality. If
\mathbb{E}[\|X\|^{k+1}]\geq 1
then
\mathbb{E}[\|X\|^{k+1}]^{\frac{k}{k+1}}\leq\mathbb{E}[\|X\|^{k+1}]
, and hence
\mathbb{E}[\|X\|^{k}]\leq\mathbb{E}[\|X\|^{k+1}]
, and if
\mathbb{E}[\|X\|^{k+1}]\leq 1
then
\mathbb{E}[\|X\|^{k}]\leq 1
, from which we establish Lemma
C.5
. Also note that from [Lemma A.2,
[
39
]
], the random variable
\|\hat{\nu}^{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}}
is finite almost surely.

Lemma C.6
.

For all
1\leq j\leq m
, let
\|\nu_{j}\|_{\mathcal{G}}\leq\sigma
, and let
\tilde{\sigma}:=\max\{\sigma,\|\hat{\nu}_{1}^{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}},\|\hat{\nu}_{2}^{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}},...,\|\hat{\nu}_{m}^{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}}\}
. Then if
\|\mu\|_{\mathcal{G}}\leq\tilde{\sigma}
,

\mathbb{E}[|OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu,\nu_{j})-OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu,\hat{\nu}^{n}_{j})|]\leq\frac{m^{1/2}P_{d,\epsilon}(\sigma)}{\sqrt{n}},\;\;\forall 1\leq j\leq m,

(61)

for some polynomial
P_{d,\epsilon}(\sigma)
with coefficients depending on
d,\epsilon
and degree depending on
d
.

Proof.

As
\mu,\nu_{j}
and
\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n}
are all
\tilde{\sigma}
subgaussian, by Proposition
C.3
we have:

\mathbb{E}[|OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu,\nu_{j})-OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu,\hat{\nu}^{n}_{j})|]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[2\sup_{u\in\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon,\tilde{\sigma}}}\bigg|\int ud\nu_{j}-\int ud\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n}\bigg|\right],

where the expectation is with respect to the empirical measure
\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n}
and
\tilde{\sigma}
. By Proposition
C.4
and Young’s inequality we may upper bound:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[2\sup_{u\in\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon,\tilde{\sigma}}}\bigg|\int ud\nu_{j}-\int ud\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n}\bigg|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2C_{d,\epsilon}}{\sqrt{n}}(\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\sigma}}[(1+\tilde{\sigma}^{3s})^{2}])^{1/2}(1+\sigma^{d+2})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2C_{d,\epsilon}}{\sqrt{n}}(2+2\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\sigma}}[\tilde{\sigma}^{6s}])^{1/2}(1+\sigma^{d+2})

(62)

where
s=\lceil\frac{d}{2}\rceil+1
. It remains to bound
\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\sigma}}[\tilde{\sigma}^{6s}]
.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\sigma}}[\tilde{\sigma}^{6s}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\max\{\sigma,\|\hat{\nu}_{1}^{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}},\|\hat{\nu}_{2}^{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}},...,\|\hat{\nu}_{m}^{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}}\}^{6s}]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\sigma^{6s}+\sum_{j=1}^{m}\|\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}}^{6s}\right]

\displaystyle=\sigma^{6s}+\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}}^{6s}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sigma^{6s}+mL_{6s}\sigma^{6s}

by Lemma
C.5
. Therefore:

\displaystyle\frac{2C_{d,\epsilon}}{\sqrt{n}}(2+2\mathbb{E}_{\tilde{\sigma}}[\tilde{\sigma}^{6s}])^{1/2}(1+\sigma^{d+2})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2C_{d,\epsilon}}{\sqrt{n}}(2+2(mL_{6s}+1)\sigma^{6s})^{1/2}(1+\sigma^{d+2})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2m^{1/2}C_{d,\epsilon}}{\sqrt{n}}(2+2(L_{6s}+1)\sigma^{6s})^{1/2}(1+\sigma^{d+2})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2m^{1/2}C_{d,\epsilon}}{\sqrt{n}}(\sqrt{2}+\sqrt{2(L_{6s}+1)}\sigma^{3s})(1+\sigma^{d+2}).

We conclude that
\mathbb{E}[|OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu,\nu_{j})-OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu,\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n})|]\leq\frac{m^{1/2}P_{d,\epsilon}(\sigma)}{\sqrt{n}}
for some polynomial
P_{d,\epsilon}(\sigma)
with coefficients depending on
d,\epsilon
and degree depending on
d
.
∎

Lemma C.7
.

Let
\mu\in\mathcal{G}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. Then:

\mathbb{E}\left[|OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu,\mu)-OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\hat{\mu}^{n},\hat{\mu}^{n})|\right]\leq\frac{2P_{d,\epsilon}(\sigma)}{\sqrt{n}},

for some polynomial
P_{d,\epsilon}(\sigma)
with coefficients depending on
d,\epsilon
and degree depending on
d
, and the expectation is over the empirical measure
\hat{\mu}^{n}.

Proof.

We apply the triangle inequality to bound:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu,\mu)-OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\hat{\mu}^{n},\hat{\mu}^{n})|\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu,\mu)-OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\hat{\mu}^{n},\mu)+OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\hat{\mu}^{n},\mu)-OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\hat{\mu}^{n},\hat{\mu}^{n})|\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu,\mu)-OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\hat{\mu}^{n},\mu)|\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\hat{\mu}^{n},\mu)-OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\hat{\mu}^{n},\hat{\mu}^{n})|\right].

(63)

The first term is bounded by
\frac{P_{d,\epsilon}(\sigma)}{\sqrt{n}}
following a similar argument as used in the proof of Lemma
C.6
with
m=1
. We bound the second term by conditioning:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\hat{\mu}^{n},\hat{\mu}^{n})-OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\hat{\mu}^{n},\mu)|\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[|OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\hat{\mu}^{n},\hat{\mu}^{n})-OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\hat{\mu}^{n},\mu)|\;\bigg|\;\hat{\mu}^{n}\right]\right]

(64)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[2\sup_{u\in\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon,\tilde{\sigma}}}\bigg|\int ud\mu-\int ud\hat{\mu}^{n}\;\bigg|\;\bigg|\;\hat{\mu}^{n}\right]\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[2\sup_{u\in\mathcal{F}_{\epsilon,\tilde{\sigma}}}\bigg|\int ud\mu-\int ud\hat{\mu}^{n}\;\bigg|\right]

where we applied Proposition
C.3
to the inner expectation in (
64
) with
\tilde{\sigma}=\max\{\sigma,\|\hat{\mu}^{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}}\}
. We then apply Proposition
C.4
followed by Lemma
C.5
to obtain the bound:

\mathbb{E}\left[|OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\hat{\mu}^{n},\hat{\mu}^{n})-OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\hat{\mu}^{n},\mu)|\right]\leq\frac{P_{d,\epsilon}(\sigma)}{\sqrt{n}}.

∎

Proof of Proposition
C.1
:

We begin by proving (
54
). Let
\mu^{*}
be a minimizer for
F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}
, and
\mu^{*,n}
be a random minimizer for
F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}
, so that

\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}F_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)-\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}F_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)\bigg|\right]=\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|F_{\lambda,{\mathcal{V}}}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{*})-F_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{*,n})\bigg|\right].

Suppose that
F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}(\mu^{*,n})\geq F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu^{*})
. Then:

\displaystyle\bigg|F_{\lambda,{\mathcal{V}}}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{*})-F_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{*,n})\bigg|

\displaystyle=F_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{*,n})-F_{\lambda,{\mathcal{V}}}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{*})

\displaystyle\leq F_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{*})-F_{\lambda,{\mathcal{V}}}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{*}).

Similarly, if
F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}(\mu^{*,n})\leq F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu^{*})
, we have

\bigg|F_{\lambda,{\mathcal{V}}}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{*})-F_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{*,n})\bigg|\leq F_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{*,n})-F_{\lambda,{\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{*,n})

and hence we may conclude that

\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|F_{\lambda,{\mathcal{V}}}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{*})-F_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{*,n})\bigg|\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{\mu\in\{\mu^{*},\mu^{*,n}\}}\bigg|F_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)-F_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)\bigg|\right].

We then bound:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{\mu\in\{\mu^{*},\mu^{*,n}\}}\bigg|F_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)-F_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)\bigg|\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{\mu\in\{\mu^{*},\mu^{*,n}\}}\bigg|\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu,\nu_{j})-\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu,\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n})\bigg|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{\mu\in\{\mu^{*},\mu^{*,n}\}}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\bigg|OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu,\nu_{j})-OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu,\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n})\bigg|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\max_{\mu\in\{\mu^{*},\mu^{*,n}\}}\bigg|OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu,\nu_{j})-OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu,\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n})\bigg|\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{\mu\in\{\mu^{*},\mu^{*,n}\}}\bigg|OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu,\nu_{j})-OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu,\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n})\bigg|\right]

(65)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu^{*},\nu_{j})-OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu^{*},\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n})\bigg|\right]+\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu^{*,n},\nu_{j})-OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu^{*,n},\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n})\bigg|\right]

(66)

To bound
\mathbb{E}\left[|OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{*},\nu_{j})-OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{*},\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n})|\right]
, note by Proposition
2.7
,
\mu^{*}
is
\sigma
-subgaussian, and
\mu^{*,n}
is
\max_{1\leq j\leq m}\{\|\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}}\}
-subgaussian, both
\mu^{*}
and
\mu^{*,n}
are
\max\{\sigma,\|\hat{\nu}^{n}_{1}\|_{\mathcal{G}},...,\|\hat{\nu}_{m}^{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}}\}
-subgaussian, and we may apply Lemma
C.6
to conclude:

\mathbb{E}\left[|OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{*},\nu_{j})-OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{*},\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n})|\right]\leq\frac{m^{1/2}P_{d,\epsilon}(\sigma)}{\sqrt{n}},\,\,\mathbb{E}\left[|OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{*,n},\nu_{j})-OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{*,n},\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n})|\right]\leq\frac{m^{1/2}P_{d,\epsilon}(\sigma)}{\sqrt{n}},

(67)

where
P_{d,\epsilon}(\sigma)
is a polynomial in
\sigma
, depending on
d
and
\epsilon
. Summing over
1\leq j\leq m
, we establish (
54
) with a bound of
\frac{2m^{1/2}P_{d,\epsilon}(\sigma)}{\sqrt{n}}.

Next we prove (
55
). Let
\mu^{*}
minimize
F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}
. Note that the empirical measure
\hat{\mu^{*}}^{n}
associated to
\mu^{*}
is an element of
\mathcal{P}^{n}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, and hence

\displaystyle\left|\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu)-\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}^{n}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu)\right|

\displaystyle=\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}^{n}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu)-\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\hat{\mu^{*}}^{n})\right]-F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu^{*})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[|F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\hat{\mu^{*}}^{n})-F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu^{*})|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\mathbb{E}\left[|OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\hat{\mu^{*}}^{n},\nu_{j})-OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{*},\nu_{j})|\right]

(68)

by the triangle inequality. We bound each term

\mathbb{E}\left[|OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\hat{\mu^{*}}^{n},\nu_{j})-OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{*},\nu_{j})|\right]\leq\frac{P_{\epsilon,d}(\sigma)}{\sqrt{n}}

by applying Lemma
C.6
with
m=1
. This applies because
\|\mu^{*}\|_{\mathcal{G}}\leq\sigma
, by Proposition
2.7
. This gives (
55
).

We now prove (
56
):

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}F_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)-\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}^{n_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}F_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n_{2}}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)\bigg|\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}F_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)-\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}F_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n_{2}}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)\bigg|\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}F_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n_{2}}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)-\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}^{n_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}F_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n_{2}}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)\bigg|\right]

We apply (
54
) to the first term to produce a bound of
\frac{2m^{1/2}P_{d,\epsilon}(\sigma)}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}
. Let

\tilde{\sigma}=\max_{1\leq j\leq m}\|\hat{\nu}^{n_{2}}_{j}\|_{\mathcal{G}}.

Then, by conditioning on the samples
\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n_{2}}
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}F_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n_{2}}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)-\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}^{n_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}F_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n_{2}}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)\bigg|\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}F_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n_{2}}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)-\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}^{n_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}F_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n_{2}}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)\bigg|\;\bigg|\;\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n_{2}}\right]\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{2P_{d,\epsilon}(\tilde{\sigma})}{\sqrt{n_{1}}}\;\bigg|\;\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n_{2}}\right]\right]

(69)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{2}{\sqrt{n_{1}}}\mathbb{E}[P_{\sigma,\epsilon}(\tilde{\sigma})].

(70)

where we applied (
55
) conditionally on the samples
\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n_{2}}
. By linearity of expectation:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[P_{d,\epsilon}(\tilde{\sigma})\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i=1}^{r_{d}}c_{d,\epsilon,i}\tilde{\sigma}^{i}\right]

(71)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=1}^{r_{d}}c_{d,\epsilon,i}\mathbb{E}(\tilde{\sigma}^{i})

(72)

for some maximum degree
r_{d}
depending on
d
, and (deterministic) coefficients
c_{d,\epsilon,i}
, depending on
d
and
\epsilon
. Since
\tilde{\sigma}^{i}\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m}\|\hat{\nu}_{j}\|_{\mathcal{G}}^{i}
, we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[P_{d,\epsilon}(\tilde{\sigma})]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{r_{d}}c_{d,\epsilon,i}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}}^{i}]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{i=1}^{r_{d}}c_{d,\epsilon,i}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\max\{L_{\lceil\frac{i}{2}\rceil}\sigma^{2\lceil{\frac{i}{2}\rceil}},1\}

\displaystyle\leq m\sum_{i=1}^{r_{d}}c_{d,\epsilon,i}(L_{\lceil\frac{i}{2}\rceil}\sigma^{2\lceil\frac{i}{2}\rceil}+1)

\displaystyle=:m\tilde{P}_{d,\epsilon}(\sigma),

where we applied Lemma
C.5
. Thus we may conclude that:

\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}F_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)-\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}^{n_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}F_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n_{2}}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)\bigg|\right]\leq(2m^{1/2}P_{d,\epsilon}(\sigma)+m\tilde{P}_{d,\epsilon}(\sigma))\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{1}}}\right),

which implies (
56
).

Labeling the maximum over all constants in the three statements in Proposition
C.1
as
R^{*}_{d,\epsilon,\sigma}
, we obtain our result. ∎

Proof of Proposition
C.2
:
We first show (
57
). Let
\mu^{*}\in\argmin_{\mu\in\mathcal{G}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}S^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}
and
\mu^{*,n}\in\argmin_{\mu\in\mathcal{G}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}S^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}
. Then we bound:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{G}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}S_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)-\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{G}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}S_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)\bigg|\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\max_{\mu\in\{\mu^{*},\mu^{*,n}\}}\bigg|OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu,\nu_{j})-OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu,\hat{\nu}^{n}_{j})-\frac{1}{2}OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\nu_{j},\nu_{j})+\frac{1}{2}OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n},\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n})\bigg|\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\max_{\mu\in\{\mu^{*},\mu^{*,n}\}}\left(\bigg|OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu,\nu_{j})-OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu,\hat{\nu}^{n}_{j})\bigg|+\bigg|\frac{1}{2}OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\nu_{j},\nu_{j})-\frac{1}{2}OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n},\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n})\bigg|\right)\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{\mu\in\{\mu^{*},\mu^{*,n}\}}|OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu,\nu_{j})-OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu,\hat{\nu}^{n}_{j})|\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[|\frac{1}{2}OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\nu_{j},\nu_{j})-\frac{1}{2}OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n},\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n})|\right]\right)

where we have applied the triangle inequality. Using the assumptions
\mu^{*},\mu^{*,n}\in\mathcal{G}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we bound:

\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{\mu\in\{\mu^{*},\mu^{*,n}\}}|OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu,\nu_{j})-OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu,\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n})|\right]\leq\frac{2m^{1/2}P_{d,\epsilon}(\sigma)}{\sqrt{n}}

(73)

by applying Lemma
C.6
. To bound
\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\mathbb{E}\left[|OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\nu_{j},\nu_{j})-OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n},\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n})|\right]
, we apply Lemma
C.7
, giving us a bound of
\frac{P_{d,\epsilon}(\sigma)}{\sqrt{n}}.
We conclude:

\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{G}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}S_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)-\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{G}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}S_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)\bigg|\right]\leq\frac{(2m^{1/2}+1)P_{d,\epsilon}(\sigma)}{\sqrt{n}},

which establishes (
57
).

Similarly, to establish (
58
), we let
\mu^{*}
again be the minimizer of
S^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}
on
\mathcal{G}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
and let
\hat{\mu^{*}}^{n}
be the empirical measure for
\mu^{*}
. By assumption, we have for any
\rho\in\argmin_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}^{n}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}S^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu)
,
\rho\in\mathcal{G}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, and hence:

\displaystyle\bigg|\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{G}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}S^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu)-\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}^{n}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}S^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu)\bigg|

\displaystyle=\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}^{n}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}S^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu)-S^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu^{*}).

Since
\hat{\mu^{*}}^{n}\in\mathcal{P}^{n}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we may upper bound this by:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[S_{\lambda,{\mathcal{V}}}^{\epsilon}(\hat{\mu^{*}}^{n})\right]-S_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{*})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|S_{\lambda,{\mathcal{V}}}^{\epsilon}(\hat{\mu^{*}}^{n})-S_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{*})\bigg|\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\mathbb{E}\left[|OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\hat{\mu^{*}}^{n},\nu_{j})-OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{*},\nu_{j})|\right]+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\left[|OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\hat{\mu^{*}}^{n},\hat{\mu^{*}}^{n})-OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{*},\mu^{*})|\right]

by the triangle inequality. By the assumption that
\mu^{*}\in\mathcal{G}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, we may apply Lemma
C.6
to bound each summand in the first term by
\frac{P_{d,\epsilon}(\sigma)}{\sqrt{n}}
and the second term can be bounded by
\frac{P_{d,\epsilon}(\sigma)}{\sqrt{n}}
via Lemma
C.7
. Combining these two bounds, we have:

\bigg|\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{G}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}S^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu)-\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}^{n}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}S^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu)\bigg|\leq\frac{2P_{d,\epsilon}(\sigma)}{\sqrt{n}}.

(74)

We conclude with the proof of (
59
):

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{G}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}S_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)-\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}^{n_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}S_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n_{2}}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)\bigg|\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\bigg|\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{G}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}S_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)-\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}^{n_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}S_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)\bigg|+\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}^{n_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}S_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)-\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}^{n_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}S_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n_{2}}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)\bigg|\right].

by the triangle inequality. We may apply (
74
) to bound the first term by
\frac{2P_{d,\epsilon}(\sigma)}{\sqrt{n_{1}}}
. To bound the second term, we apply a similar argument as used to bound (
57
). Let
\mu^{n_{1}}\in\argmin_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}^{n_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}S^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu)
and
\mu^{n_{1}}_{n_{2}}\in\argmin_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}^{n_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}S^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n_{2}}}(\mu)
. Then:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}^{n_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}S^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu)-\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}^{n_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}S^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n_{2}}}(\mu)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{\mu^{*}\in\{\mu^{n_{1}},\mu^{n_{1}}_{n_{2}}\}}\bigg|S^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu^{*})-S^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n_{2}}}(\mu^{*})\bigg|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{\mu^{*}\in\{\mu^{n_{1}},\mu^{n_{1}}_{n_{2}}\}}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}(|OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{*},\nu_{j})-OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{*},\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n_{2}})|\right]+\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\mathbb{E}\left[|OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\nu_{j},\nu_{j})-OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n_{2}},\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n_{2}})|\right]

by the triangle inequality. The second term can be bounded by
\frac{P_{d,\epsilon}(\sigma)}{\sqrt{n}}
by Lemma
C.7
. To deal with the first term, note that by assumption,
\mu^{n_{1}}\in\mathcal{G}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, and
\mu^{n_{1}}_{n_{2}}\in\mathcal{G}_{\tilde{\sigma}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
with
\tilde{\sigma}=\max_{1\leq j\leq m}\{\|\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n_{2}}\|\}
, and hence
\{\mu^{n_{1}},\mu^{n_{1}}_{n_{2}}\}\subset\mathcal{G}_{\sigma^{*}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
where
\sigma^{*}=\max\{\sigma,\tilde{\sigma}\}
. Hence we can bound:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\max_{\mu^{*}\in\{\mu^{n_{1}},\mu^{n_{1}}_{n_{2}}\}}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}|OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{*},\nu_{j})-OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{*},\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n_{2}})|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\mathbb{E}\left[|OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{n_{1}},\nu_{j})-OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{n_{1}},\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n_{2}})|\right]+\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\mathbb{E}\left[|OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{n_{1}}_{n_{2}},\nu_{j})-OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{n_{1}}_{n_{2}},\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n_{2}})|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\frac{m^{1/2}P_{d,\epsilon}(\sigma)}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}+\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\frac{m^{1/2}P_{d,\epsilon}(\sigma)}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{2m^{1/2}P_{d,\epsilon}(\sigma)}{\sqrt{n_{2}}},

where we applied Lemma
C.6
in the final inequality. Thus we have bounded

\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{G}_{\sigma}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}S_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)-\min_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}^{n_{1}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}S_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n_{2}}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)\bigg|\right]\leq 2(m^{1/2}+1)P_{d,\epsilon}(\sigma)\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{1}}}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{n_{2}}}\right).

Taking the maximum over all constants as
R^{*}_{d,\epsilon,\sigma}
, we conclude.

Proof of Theorem
3.1
:
Let
\mu^{*}=\argmin_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu)
, and let
\mu^{n}
denote a random minimizer of
F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}^{n}}}
over
\mathcal{P}^{n}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
. We bound:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu^{*})-F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu^{n})\bigg|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu^{*})-F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}(\mu^{n})\bigg|\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}(\mu^{n})-F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu^{n})\bigg|\right].

By Proposition
C.1
, specifically (
56
), the first term is upper bounded by
\frac{mR^{*}_{d,\epsilon,\sigma}}{\sqrt{n}}.
We bound the second term:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}(\mu^{n})-F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu^{n})\bigg|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\mathbb{E}\left[|OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{n},\hat{\nu}^{n}_{j})-OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu^{n},\nu_{j})|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\frac{m^{1/2}P_{d,\epsilon}(\sigma)}{\sqrt{n}}

\displaystyle=\frac{m^{1/2}P_{d,\epsilon}(\sigma)}{\sqrt{n}}.

Above, we applied the triangle inequality, followed by (
61
) from Lemma
C.6
to each summand, which applies since
\|\mu^{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}}\leq\max_{1\leq j\leq m}\|\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}}
almost-surely. From this we conclude (
9
), with a final bound of
\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}(m^{1/2}P_{d,\epsilon}(\sigma)+mR^{*}_{d,\epsilon,\sigma})
.

Similar arguments apply to
S^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}
. By assumption, for
\mu^{n}\in\argmin_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}^{n}(\mathbb{R}^{d})}S^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}(\mu)
we have
\|\mu^{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}}\leq\max_{1\leq j\leq m}\{\|\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n}\|_{\mathcal{G}},\sigma\}=:\tilde{\sigma}.
We bound:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|S^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu^{*})-S^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu^{n})\bigg|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|S^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu^{*})-S^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}(\mu^{n})\bigg|\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|S^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}(\mu^{n})-S^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu^{n})\bigg|\right].

We apply (
59
) to bound the first term by
\frac{mR^{*}_{d,\epsilon,\sigma}}{\sqrt{n}}
. We bound the second term:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|S^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}(\mu^{n})-S^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu^{n})\bigg|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\mathbb{E}\left[|OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{n},\hat{\nu}^{n}_{j})-OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu^{n},\nu_{j})|+\frac{1}{2}|OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\nu_{j},\nu_{j})-OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n},\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n})|\right]

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\mathbb{E}\left[|OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\mu^{n},\hat{\nu}^{n}_{j})-OT^{\epsilon}_{2}(\mu^{n},\nu_{j})|\right]+\frac{1}{2}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\mathbb{E}\left[|OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\nu_{j},\nu_{j})-OT_{2}^{\epsilon}(\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n},\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n})|\right]

by the triangle inequality. To bound the first term, we apply (
61
) from Lemma
C.6
to each summand, which applies since
\mu^{n}\in\mathcal{G}_{\tilde{\sigma}}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
, giving us a bound of
\frac{m^{1/2}P_{d,\epsilon}(\sigma)}{\sqrt{n}}
To bound the second term, we apply Lemma
C.7
, giving a bound of
\frac{P_{d,\epsilon}(\sigma)}{\sqrt{n}}
. Adding these bounds together, we have:

\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|S^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu^{*})-S^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu^{n})\bigg|\right]\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}(mR^{*}_{d,\epsilon,\sigma}+(m^{1/2}+1)P_{d,\epsilon}(\sigma)).

Taking the maximum over all constants depending on
d,\epsilon
and
\sigma
as
C^{*}_{d,\epsilon,\sigma},
we conclude.
∎

Appendix D
Proofs for Section
4

Proof of Proposition
4.1
:
The assumption that
\mathcal{V}\subset\mathcal{G}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
ensures that
\nabla\delta F_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)
exists and equals
Id-\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{j}}
. We compute:

\displaystyle\|\nabla\delta F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu)\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|Id-\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{j}}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mu)}

\displaystyle=\int\langle Id-\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{j}},Id-\sum_{k=1}^{m}\lambda_{k}T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{k}}\rangle d\mu

\displaystyle=\int\sum_{j,k=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\lambda_{k}\langle Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{j}},Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{k}}\rangle d\mu

\displaystyle=\sum_{j,k=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\lambda_{k}[A_{\mu}^{\epsilon}]_{jk}=\lambda^{\top}A_{\mu}^{\epsilon}\lambda.

Similarly:

\displaystyle\|\nabla\delta S^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\tilde{\mu})\|_{L^{2}(\tilde{\mu})}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|T^{\epsilon}_{{\tilde{\mu}}\rightarrow{\tilde{\mu}}}-\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}T^{\epsilon}_{\tilde{\mu}\rightarrow\nu_{j}}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\tilde{\mu})}

\displaystyle=\int\langle T^{\epsilon}_{{\tilde{\mu}}\rightarrow{\tilde{\mu}}}-\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}T^{\epsilon}_{\tilde{\mu}\rightarrow\nu_{j}},T^{\epsilon}_{{\tilde{\mu}}\rightarrow{\tilde{\mu}}}-\sum_{k=1}^{m}\lambda_{k}T^{\epsilon}_{\tilde{\mu}\rightarrow\nu_{k}}\rangle d\tilde{\mu}

\displaystyle=\int\sum_{j,k=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\lambda_{k}\langle T^{\epsilon}_{{\tilde{\mu}}\rightarrow{\tilde{\mu}}}-T^{\epsilon}_{\tilde{\mu}\rightarrow\nu_{j}},T^{\epsilon}_{{\tilde{\mu}}\rightarrow{\tilde{\mu}}}-T^{\epsilon}_{\tilde{\mu}\rightarrow\nu_{k}}\rangle d\tilde{\mu}

\displaystyle=\sum_{j,k=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\lambda_{k}[S_{\tilde{\mu}}^{\epsilon}]_{jk}=\lambda^{\top}S_{\tilde{\mu}}^{\epsilon}\lambda.

Hence we see that
\lambda^{\top}A^{\epsilon}_{\mu}\lambda\geq 0,
and
\lambda^{\top}A_{\mu}^{\epsilon}\lambda=0
is equivalent to
\|\nabla\delta F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mu)}=0
, which is itself equivalent to
\nabla\delta F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu)=0

\mu
-a.e. The analogous result holds for
S^{\epsilon}_{\tilde{\mu}}
. Finally, if
\texttt{supp}(\mu)=\Omega
(resp.
\texttt{supp}(\tilde{\mu})=\Omega
) is connected, then we may apply the optimality criteria from Corollary
2.5
.

∎

D.1
Proof of Theorem
4.2

Lemma D.1
.

Let
\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\Omega)
and
\mathcal{V}\subset\mathcal{G}_{\sigma}(\Omega)
, and suppose that
A_{\mu}^{\epsilon}
has an eigenvalue of 0 with unique eigenvector
\lambda_{*}\in\Delta^{m}
. Suppose there exists an estimator
\hat{T}(\hat{\mu}^{n},\hat{\nu}^{n}_{j})
satisfying (
11
) for
1\leq j\leq m
. Then:

\mathbb{E}[|[A_{\mu}^{\epsilon}]_{ij}-[\hat{A}^{\epsilon}_{\mu}]_{ij}|]\leq Jd\sigma^{2}\max\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}},\sqrt{\theta(n)}+\sqrt{\theta(n)+\theta(n)^{2}}\right\}

where
\hat{A}_{\mu}
is the matrix
\hat{M}_{\mu}
in Algorithm
1
with
\mathcal{F}^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}=F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}
, and
J
is an absolute constant.

Proof.

For ease of notation, we will write
T^{\epsilon}_{j}
for
T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{j}}
and
\hat{T_{j}^{\epsilon}}
for
\hat{T}(\hat{\mu}^{n},\hat{\nu}^{n}_{j})
. We begin by bounding
\mathbb{E}[|[A_{\mu}^{\epsilon}]_{ij}-[\hat{A}^{\epsilon}_{\mu}]_{ij}|]
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|[A_{\mu}^{\epsilon}]_{ij}-[\hat{A}^{\epsilon}_{\mu}]_{ij}|]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\int\langle T^{\epsilon}_{j}-Id,T^{\epsilon}_{i}-Id\rangle d\mu-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=n+1}^{2n}\langle\hat{T_{j}^{\epsilon}}(X_{k})-X_{k},\hat{T_{i}^{\epsilon}}(X_{k})-X_{k}\rangle\bigg|\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\int\langle T^{\epsilon}_{j}-Id,T^{\epsilon}_{i}-Id\rangle d\mu-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=n+1}^{2n}\langle T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X_{k})-X_{k},T^{\epsilon}_{i}(X_{k})-X_{k}\rangle\bigg|\right]

(75)
