\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=n+1}^{2n}\langle T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X_{k})-X_{k},T^{\epsilon}_{i}(X_{k})-X_{k}\rangle-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=n+1}^{2n}\langle\hat{T^{\epsilon}_{j}}(X_{k})-X_{k},\hat{T_{i}^{\epsilon}}(X_{k})-X_{k}\rangle\bigg|\right]

(76)

The term (
75
) may be bounded by observing that
\langle T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X_{k})-X_{k},T^{\epsilon}_{i}(X_{k})-X_{k}\rangle:=Z_{k}
are i.i.d. samples from the random variable
\langle T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X)-X,T^{\epsilon}_{i}(X)-X\rangle:=Z
with
X\sim\mu
. Using Jensen’s inequality and the independence of
Z_{n+1},Z_{n+2},...,Z_{2n}
, we bound:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{Z_{n+1},...,Z_{2n}}\left[\left|\mathbb{E}_{Z}[Z]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=n+1}^{2n}Z_{k}\right|\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{Z_{n+1},...,Z_{2n}}\left[\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=n+1}^{2n}(\mathbb{E}_{Z}[Z]-Z_{k})\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{Z_{n+1},...,Z_{2n}}\left[\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=n+1}^{2n}(\mathbb{E}_{Z}[Z]-Z_{k})\right|^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{1}{n^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{Z_{n+1},...,Z_{2n}}\left[\sum_{k=n+1}^{2n}\sum_{k^{\prime}=n+1}^{2n}(\mathbb{E}_{Z}[Z]-Z_{k})(\mathbb{E}_{Z}[Z]-Z_{k^{\prime}})\right]}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{1}{n^{2}}\mathbb{E}_{Z_{n+1},...,Z_{2n}}\left[\sum_{k=n+1}^{2n}(\mathbb{E}_{Z}[Z]-Z_{k})^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle=\sqrt{\frac{\mathbb{E}_{Z_{n+1}}[(\mathbb{E}_{Z}[Z]-Z_{n+1})^{2}]}{n}}

where the outer expectation in the last line is over the sample
Z_{n+1}
. Observe that
\mathbb{E}_{Z_{n+1}}(\mathbb{E}_{Z}[Z]-Z_{n+1})^{2}=\mathbb{E}_{Z}(\mathbb{E}_{Z}[Z]-Z)^{2}
is the variance of the random variable
Z
. Hence we may bound:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}_{Z}[(\mathbb{E}_{Z}[Z]-Z)^{2}]

(77)

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{Z}[Z^{2}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}[\langle T_{i}^{\epsilon}(X)-X,T_{j}^{\epsilon}(X)-X\rangle^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}[\|T_{i}^{\epsilon}(X)-X\|^{2}\|T_{j}^{\epsilon}(X)-X\|^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\mathbb{E}[\|T_{i}^{\epsilon}(X)-X\|^{4}]\mathbb{E}[\|T_{j}^{\epsilon}(X)-X\|^{4}]\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq(\mathbb{E}[(\|T_{i}^{\epsilon}(X)\|+\|X\|)^{4}]\mathbb{E}[(\|T_{j}^{\epsilon}(X)\|+\|X\|)^{4}])^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\left(2^{4}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\|T_{i}^{\epsilon}(X)\|}{2}+\frac{\|X\|}{2}\right)^{4}\right]\cdot 2^{4}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\frac{\|T_{j}^{\epsilon}(X)\|}{2}+\frac{\|X\|}{2}\right)^{4}\right]\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(2^{8}\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{2}(\|T_{i}^{\epsilon}(X)\|^{4}+\|X\|^{4})\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\frac{1}{2}(\|T_{j}^{\epsilon}(X)\|^{4}+\|X\|^{4})\right]\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\left(2^{6}(\mathbb{E}[\|T_{i}^{\epsilon}(X)\|^{4}]+\mathbb{E}[\|X\|^{4}])(\mathbb{E}[\|T_{j}^{\epsilon}(X)\|^{4}]+\mathbb{E}[\|X\|^{4}])\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=8\left((\mathbb{E}[\|T_{i}^{\epsilon}(X)\|^{4}]+\mathbb{E}[\|X\|^{4}])(\mathbb{E}[\|T_{j}^{\epsilon}(X)\|^{4}]+\mathbb{E}[\|X\|^{4}])\right)^{1/2}

(78)

where we applied Jensen’s inequality, two applications of the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, the triangle inequality, and Jensen’s inequality. By Lemma
B.22
, we have that

\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\mu}[\|T_{i}^{\epsilon}(X)\|^{4}]\leq\mathbb{E}_{Y\sim\nu_{i}}[\|Y\|^{4}],\;\;\;\forall 1\leq i\leq m.

Furthermore, as
\mu
is a critical point, we may apply Proposition
B.23
to bound

\mathbb{E}_{X\sim\mu}[\|X\|^{4}]\leq\mathbb{E}_{Y\sim\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\nu_{j}}[\|Y\|^{4}]

and combining these we have upper bounded (
78
) by
16\max_{k}\mathbb{E}_{Y\sim\nu_{k}}(\|Y\|^{4}).
As
\mathcal{V}\subset\mathcal{G}_{\sigma}(\Omega),
by Lemma
A.2
,
\|Y\|
is
q_{G}\sqrt{d}\sigma
-subgaussian. Hence by applying item 3 in Definition
A.1
, we have the upper bound

\displaystyle 16\max_{k}\mathbb{E}_{Y\sim\nu_{k}}(\|Y\|^{4})

\displaystyle\leq 16(2C_{G}q_{G}\sqrt{d}\sigma)^{4}

\displaystyle=2^{8}C_{G}^{4}q_{G}^{4}d^{2}\sigma^{4}

Hence we have upper bounded
75
:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[|\mathbb{E}[Z]-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=n+1}^{2n}Z_{k}|\right]\leq\frac{16C_{G}^{2}q_{G}^{2}d\sigma^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}.

(79)

We now bound (
76
):

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=n+1}^{2n}\langle T^{\epsilon}_{i}(X_{k})-X_{k},T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X_{k})-X_{k}\rangle-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=n+1}^{2n}\langle\hat{T^{\epsilon}_{i}}(X_{k})-X_{k},\hat{T_{j}^{\epsilon}}(X_{k})-X_{k}\rangle\bigg|\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=n+1}^{2n}\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\langle T^{\epsilon}_{i}(X_{k})-X_{k},T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X_{k})-X_{k}\rangle-\langle\hat{T^{\epsilon}_{i}}(X_{k})-X_{k},\hat{T_{j}^{\epsilon}}(X_{k})-X_{k}\rangle\bigg|\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\langle T^{\epsilon}_{i}(X_{k})-X_{k},T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X_{k})-X_{k}\rangle-\langle\hat{T^{\epsilon}_{i}}(X_{k})-X_{k},\hat{T^{\epsilon}_{j}}(X_{k})-X_{k}\rangle\bigg|\right]

for an arbitrary
k\in\{n+1,n+2,...,2n\}
, where we have used the triangle inequality and the fact that
X_{k}
are identically distributed. Now,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\langle T^{\epsilon}_{i}(X_{k})-X_{k},T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X_{k})-X_{k}\rangle-\langle\hat{T^{\epsilon}_{i}}(X_{k})-X_{k},\hat{T_{j}^{\epsilon}}(X_{k})-X_{k}\rangle\bigg|\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\langle T^{\epsilon}_{i}(X_{k})-X_{k},T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X_{k})-X_{k}\rangle-\langle\hat{T^{\epsilon}_{i}}(X_{k})-X_{k},T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X_{k})-X_{k}\rangle\bigg|\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\langle\hat{T^{\epsilon}_{i}}(X_{k})-X_{k},T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X_{k})-X_{k}\rangle-\langle\hat{T^{\epsilon}_{i}}(X_{k})-X_{k},\hat{T_{j}^{\epsilon}}(X_{k})-X_{k}\rangle\bigg|\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\langle T^{\epsilon}_{i}(X_{k})-\hat{T^{\epsilon}_{i}}(X_{k}),T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X_{k})-X_{k}\rangle\bigg|\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\langle\hat{T^{\epsilon}_{i}}(X_{k})-X_{k},T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X_{k})-\hat{T_{j}^{\epsilon}}(X_{k})\rangle\bigg|\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|T^{\epsilon}_{i}(X_{k})-\hat{T^{\epsilon}_{i}}(X_{k})\|\;\|T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X_{k})-X_{k}\|\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{T^{\epsilon}_{i}}(X_{k})-X_{k}\|\;\|T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X_{k})-\hat{T_{j}^{\epsilon}}(X_{k})\|\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\|T^{\epsilon}_{i}(X_{k})-\hat{T^{\epsilon}_{i}}(X_{k})\|^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\|T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X_{k})-X_{k}\|^{2}\right]}+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{T^{\epsilon}_{i}}(X_{k})-X_{k}\|^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\|T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X_{k})-\hat{T_{j}^{\epsilon}}(X_{k})\|^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{2\mathbb{E}\left[\|T^{\epsilon}_{i}(X_{k})-\hat{T^{\epsilon}_{i}}(X_{k})\|^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\|T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X_{k})\|^{2}+\|X_{k}\|^{2}\right]}+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{T^{\epsilon}_{i}}(X_{k})-X_{k}\|^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\|T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X_{k})-\hat{T_{j}^{\epsilon}}(X_{k})\|^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{2\mathbb{E}\left[\|T^{\epsilon}_{i}(X_{k})-\hat{T^{\epsilon}_{i}}(X_{k})\|^{2}\right]\left(M_{2}(\nu_{j})+M_{2}(\mu)\right)}+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{T^{\epsilon}_{i}}(X_{k})-X_{k}\|^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\|T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X_{k})-\hat{T_{j}^{\epsilon}}(X_{k})\|^{2}\right]}

(80)

In the above, we applied the triangle inequality, two applications of the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, the triangle inequality, Young’s inequality and Lemma
B.22
to bound
\mathbb{E}\left[\|T_{j}^{\epsilon}(X_{k})\|^{2}\right]\leq M_{2}(\nu_{j})
. We now control:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{i}(X_{k})-X_{k}\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[(\|\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{i}(X_{k})-T_{i}^{\epsilon}(X_{k})\|+\|T^{\epsilon}_{i}(X_{k})-X_{k}\|)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{i}(X_{k})-T_{i}^{\epsilon}(X_{k})\|^{2}+\|T^{\epsilon}_{i}(X_{k})-X_{k}\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{i}(X_{k})-T_{i}^{\epsilon}(X_{k})\|^{2}\right]+4\mathbb{E}\left[\|T^{\epsilon}_{i}(X_{k})\|^{2}+\|X_{k}\|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{i}(X_{k})-T_{i}^{\epsilon}(X_{k})\|^{2}\right]+4M_{2}(\nu_{i})+4M_{2}(\mu)

where we applied the triangle inequality and Young’s inequality twice, and the fact that
\mathbb{E}\left[\|T_{i}^{\epsilon}(X_{k})\|^{2}\right]\leq M_{2}(\nu_{i})
. Since
\mu
is a critical point of
F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}
, we may apply Proposition
B.23
to further bound
M_{2}(\mu)\leq\max_{1\leq k\leq m}M_{2}(\nu_{k})
. Summarizing, we have bounded (
80
) by:

\displaystyle\sqrt{2\mathbb{E}\left[\|T^{\epsilon}_{i}(X_{k})-\hat{T^{\epsilon}_{i}}(X_{k})\|^{2}\right]\left(M_{2}(\nu_{j})+\max_{1\leq k\leq m}M_{2}(\nu_{j})\right)}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sqrt{\left(2\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{i}(X_{k})-T_{i}^{\epsilon}(X_{k})\|^{2}\right]+4M_{2}(\nu_{i})+4\max_{1\leq k\leq m}M_{2}(\nu_{k})\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\|T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X_{k})-\hat{T_{j}^{\epsilon}}(X_{k})\|^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{4C_{G}^{2}q_{G}^{2}d\sigma^{2}\mathbb{E}[\|T_{i}^{\epsilon}(X_{k})-\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{i}(X_{k})\|^{2}]}

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\sqrt{2\left(\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{i}(X_{k})-T_{i}^{\epsilon}(X_{k})\|^{2}\right]+16C_{G}^{2}q_{G}^{2}d\sigma^{2}\right)\mathbb{E}[\|T_{j}^{\epsilon}(X_{k})-\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{j}(X_{k})\|^{2}]}

where we have applied Lemma
A.2
followed by item 3 in Definition
A.1
to upper bound the second moments.

Since
X_{k}\sim\mu
for
k\geq n+1
is independent of the samples
X_{1},...,X_{n}
used to compute the maps
\hat{T^{\epsilon}_{i}}
and
\hat{T_{j}^{\epsilon}}
, we may apply (
11
) to obtain the upper bound (
80
) by:

2C_{G}q_{G}\sqrt{d}\sigma\sqrt{\theta(n)}+\sqrt{2\theta(n)^{2}+16C_{G}^{2}q_{G}^{2}d\sigma^{2}\theta(n)}.

Combining this with the bound on (
75
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|[A_{\mu}^{\epsilon}]_{ij}-[\hat{A}_{\mu}^{\epsilon}]_{ij}\bigg|\right]

\displaystyle\leq Jd\sigma^{2}\max\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}},\sqrt{\theta(n)}+\sqrt{\theta(n)+\theta(n)^{2}}\right\},

(81)

where
J
is an absolute constant.
∎

We prove a similar lemma for
S^{\epsilon}_{\mu}:

Lemma D.2
.

Let
\mu\in\mathcal{G}_{\sigma}(\Omega)
and
\mathcal{V}\subset\mathcal{G}_{\sigma}(\Omega)
, and suppose that
S_{\mu}^{\epsilon}
has an eigenvalue of 0 with unique eigenvector
\lambda_{*}\in\Delta^{m}
. Suppose there exists an estimator
\hat{T}(\hat{\mu}^{n},\hat{\nu}^{n}_{j})
satisfying (
11
) for all
1\leq j\leq m
, and an estimator
\hat{T}(\hat{\mu}^{n})
satisfying (
13
). Then:

\mathbb{E}[|[S_{\mu}^{\epsilon}]_{ij}-[\hat{S}^{\epsilon}_{\mu}]_{ij}|]\leq\tilde{J}d\sigma^{2}\max\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}},\sqrt{\theta(n)}+\sqrt{\theta(n)+\theta(n)^{2}}\right\}

where
\hat{S}_{\mu}
is the matrix
\hat{M}_{\mu}
in Algorithm
1
with
\mathcal{F}^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}=S^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}
, and
\tilde{J}
is an absolute constant.

Proof.

For ease of notation, we write
T^{\epsilon}_{j}:=T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{j}}
,
\hat{T_{j}^{\epsilon}}:=\hat{T}(\hat{\mu}^{n},\hat{\nu}^{n}_{j})
,
T^{\epsilon}:=T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\mu}
and
\hat{T}^{\epsilon}:=\hat{T}(\hat{\mu}^{n})
. We bound:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\int\langle T_{i}^{\epsilon}-T^{\epsilon},T_{j}^{\epsilon}-T^{\epsilon}\rangle d\mu-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=n+1}^{2n}\langle\hat{T}_{i}^{\epsilon}(X_{k})-\hat{T}^{\epsilon}(X_{k}),\hat{T}_{j}^{\epsilon}(X_{k})-\hat{T}^{\epsilon}(X_{k})\rangle\bigg|\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\int\langle T_{i}^{\epsilon}-T^{\epsilon},T_{j}^{\epsilon}-T^{\epsilon}\rangle d\mu-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=n+1}^{2n}\langle T_{i}^{\epsilon}(X_{k})-T^{\epsilon}(X_{k}),T_{j}^{\epsilon}(X_{k})-T^{\epsilon}(X_{k})\rangle\bigg|\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=n+1}^{2n}\langle T_{i}^{\epsilon}(X_{k})-T^{\epsilon}(X_{k}),T_{j}^{\epsilon}(X_{k})-T^{\epsilon}(X_{k})\rangle-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=n+1}^{2n}\langle\hat{T}_{i}^{\epsilon}(X_{k})-T^{\epsilon}(X_{k}),\hat{T}_{j}^{\epsilon}(X_{k})-T^{\epsilon}(X_{k})\rangle\bigg|\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=n+1}^{2n}\langle\hat{T}_{i}^{\epsilon}(X_{k})-T^{\epsilon}(X_{k}),\hat{T}_{j}^{\epsilon}(X_{k})-T^{\epsilon}(X_{k})\rangle-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=n+1}^{2n}\langle\hat{T}_{i}^{\epsilon}(X_{k})-\hat{T}^{\epsilon}(X_{k}),\hat{T}_{j}^{\epsilon}(X_{k})-\hat{T}^{\epsilon}(X_{k})\rangle\bigg|\right].

(82)

By the same sequence of steps as used to bound (
75
) by (
78
), we may upper bound the first term in (
82
) by

\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}}\sqrt{8\left((\mathbb{E}[\|T^{\epsilon}_{i}(X)\|^{4}]+\mathbb{E}[\|T^{\epsilon}(X)\|^{4}])(\mathbb{E}[\|T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X)\|^{4}]+\mathbb{E}[\|T^{\epsilon}(X)\|^{4}])\right)^{1/2}}.

(83)

By Lemma
B.22
,
[T^{\epsilon}_{i}]_{\#}(\mu)\leq_{c}\nu_{i}\in\mathcal{G}_{\sigma}(\Omega)
for all
1\leq i\leq m
, and
[T^{\epsilon}]_{\#}(\mu)\leq_{c}\mu\in\mathcal{G}_{\sigma}(\Omega)
. Hence we may apply Lemma
B.21
, Lemma
A.2
and item 3 in Definition
A.1
as in the proof of Lemma
D.1
to conclude that (
83
) is upper bounded by
\frac{16C_{G}^{2}q_{G}^{2}d\sigma^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}
.

The second term in (
82
) may be bounded in the same manner as (
76
). By the triangle inequality and noting that
X_{1},X_{2},\dots,X_{2n}
are all i.i.d. we have:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=n+1}^{2n}\langle T_{i}^{\epsilon}(X_{k})-T^{\epsilon}(X_{k}),T_{j}^{\epsilon}(X_{k})-T^{\epsilon}(X_{k})\rangle-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=n+1}^{2n}\langle\hat{T}_{i}^{\epsilon}(X_{k})-T^{\epsilon}(X_{k}),\hat{T}_{j}^{\epsilon}(X_{k})-T^{\epsilon}(X_{k})\rangle\bigg|\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k={n+1}}^{2n}\mathbb{E}\left[|\langle T_{i}^{\epsilon}(X_{k})-T^{\epsilon}(X_{k}),T_{j}^{\epsilon}(X_{k})-T^{\epsilon}(X_{k})\rangle-\langle\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{i}(X_{k})-T^{\epsilon}(X_{k}),\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{j}(X_{k})-T^{\epsilon}(X_{k})\rangle|\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\langle T^{\epsilon}_{i}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X),T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X)\rangle-\langle\hat{T^{\epsilon}_{i}}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X),\hat{T^{\epsilon}_{j}}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X)\rangle\bigg|\right]

where
X\sim\mu
is independent of
X_{1},X_{2},...,X_{n}
. By the triangle inequality, we may upper bound this by:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\langle T^{\epsilon}_{i}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X),T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X)\rangle-\langle\hat{T^{\epsilon}_{i}}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X),T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X)\rangle\bigg|\right]

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\langle\hat{T^{\epsilon}_{i}}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X),T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X)\rangle-\langle\hat{T^{\epsilon}_{i}}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X),\hat{T_{j}^{\epsilon}}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X)\rangle\bigg|\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\langle T_{i}^{\epsilon}(X)-\hat{T_{i}^{\epsilon}}(X),T_{j}^{\epsilon}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X)\rangle\bigg|\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\langle\hat{T^{\epsilon}_{i}}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X),T_{j}^{\epsilon}(X)-\hat{T_{j}^{\epsilon}}(X)\rangle\bigg|\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|T_{i}^{\epsilon}(X)-\hat{T_{i}^{\epsilon}}(X)\|\|T_{j}^{\epsilon}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X)\|\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{T^{\epsilon}_{i}}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X)\|\|T_{j}^{\epsilon}(X)-\hat{T_{j}^{\epsilon}}(X)\|\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|T_{i}^{\epsilon}(X)-\hat{T_{i}^{\epsilon}}(X)\|\left(\|T_{j}^{\epsilon}(X)\|+\|T^{\epsilon}(X)\|\right)\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{T^{\epsilon}_{i}}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X)\|\|T_{j}^{\epsilon}(X)-\hat{T_{j}^{\epsilon}}(X)\|\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\|T^{\epsilon}_{i}(X)-\hat{T^{\epsilon}_{i}}(X)\|^{2}\right]\left(2\mathbb{E}[\|T_{j}^{\epsilon}(X)\|^{2}]+2\mathbb{E}[\|T^{\epsilon}(X)\|^{2}]\right)}

\displaystyle\;\;\;+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{T^{\epsilon}_{i}}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X)\|^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\|T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X)-\hat{T_{j}^{\epsilon}}(X)\|^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{2\theta(n)\left(M_{2}(\nu_{j})+\mathbb{E}[\|T^{\epsilon}(X)\|^{2}]\right)}

\displaystyle\;\;\;+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{T^{\epsilon}_{i}}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X)\|^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\|T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X)-\hat{T_{j}^{\epsilon}}(X)\|^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{2\theta(n)\left(M_{2}(\nu_{j})+\mathbb{E}[\|T^{\epsilon}(X)\|^{2}]\right)}

\displaystyle\;\;\;+\sqrt{2\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{T^{\epsilon}_{i}}(X)-T^{\epsilon}_{i}(X)\|^{2}+\|T^{\epsilon}_{i}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X)\|^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\|T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X)-\hat{T_{j}^{\epsilon}}(X)\|^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{2\theta(n)\left(M_{2}(\nu_{j})+\mathbb{E}[\|T^{\epsilon}(X)\|^{2}]\right)}

\displaystyle\;\;\;+\sqrt{2\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{T^{\epsilon}_{i}}(X)-T^{\epsilon}_{i}(X)\|^{2}+2\|T^{\epsilon}_{i}(X)\|^{2}+2\|T^{\epsilon}(X)\|^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\|T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X)-\hat{T_{j}^{\epsilon}}(X)\|^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sqrt{2\theta(n)\left(M_{2}(\nu_{j})+M_{2}(\mu)\right)}

\displaystyle\;\;\;+\sqrt{\left(2\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{i}(X)-T_{i}^{\epsilon}(X_{n})\|^{2}\right]+4M_{2}(\nu_{\ell})+4M_{2}(\mu)\right)\mathbb{E}\left[\|T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X)-\hat{T_{j}^{\epsilon}}(X)\|^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\max_{1\leq\ell\leq m}\sqrt{2(M_{2}(\nu_{j})+M_{2}(\mu))\theta(n)}+\sqrt{(2\theta(n)+4M_{2}(\nu_{\ell})+4M_{2}(\mu))\theta(n)}.

By the triangle inequality and using the fact that
X_{k},k\in\{1,\cdots,2n\}
are all i.i.d, the third term in (
82
) is upper bounded by:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\langle\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{i}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X),\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{j}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X)\rangle-\langle\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{i}(X)-\hat{T}^{\epsilon}(X),\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{j}(X)-\hat{T}^{\epsilon}(X)\rangle\right|\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[|\langle\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{i}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X),\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{j}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X)\rangle-\langle\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{i}(X)-\hat{T}^{\epsilon}(X),\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{j}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X)\rangle

\displaystyle\;\;\;+\langle\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{i}(X)-\hat{T}^{\epsilon}(X),\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{j}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X)\rangle-\langle\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{i}(X)-\hat{T}^{\epsilon}(X),\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{j}(X)-\hat{T}^{\epsilon}(X)\rangle|]

where
X\sim\mu
is independent of
X_{1},X_{2},...,X_{n}
. By the triangle inequality, we may bound this as:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\langle\hat{T}^{\epsilon}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X),\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{j}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X)\rangle\right|\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\left|\langle\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{i}(X)-\hat{T}^{\epsilon}(X),\hat{T}^{\epsilon}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X)\right|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{T}^{\epsilon}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X)\|\|\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{j}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X)\|\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{i}(X)-\hat{T}^{\epsilon}(X)\|\|\hat{T}^{\epsilon}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X)\|\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{T}^{\epsilon}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X)\|\|\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{j}(X)-T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X)+T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X)\|\right]

\displaystyle\;\;\;\;\;+\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{i}(X)-T^{\epsilon}_{i}(X)+T^{\epsilon}_{i}(X)-\hat{T}^{\epsilon}(X)\|\|\hat{T}^{\epsilon}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X)\|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{T}^{\epsilon}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X)\|\left(\|\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{j}(X)-T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X)\|+\|T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X)\|\right)\right]

\displaystyle\;\;\;\;+\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\|\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{i}(X)-T^{\epsilon}_{i}(X)\|+\|T^{\epsilon}_{i}(X)-\hat{T}^{\epsilon}(X)\|\right)\|\hat{T}^{\epsilon}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X)\|\right]

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{T}^{\epsilon}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X)\|^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[2\|\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{j}(X)-T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X)\|^{2}+2\|T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X)\|^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle\;\;\;\;\;+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[2\|\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{i}(X)-T^{\epsilon}_{i}(X)\|^{2}+2\|T_{i}^{\epsilon}(X)-\hat{T}^{\epsilon}(X)\|^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{T}^{\epsilon}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X)\|^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{T}^{\epsilon}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X)\|^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[2\|\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{j}(X)-T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X)\|^{2}+4\|T^{\epsilon}_{j}(X)\|^{2}+4\|T^{\epsilon}(X)\|^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle\;\;\;\;\;+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[2\|\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{i}(X)-T^{\epsilon}_{i}(X)\|^{2}+2\|T_{i}^{\epsilon}(X)-\hat{T}^{\epsilon}(X)\|^{2}\right]\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{T}^{\epsilon}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X)\|^{2}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2\theta(n)\left(2\theta(n)+4M_{2}(\nu_{j})+4M_{2}(\mu)\right)}

\displaystyle\;\;\;\;\;+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(2\|\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{i}(X)-T^{\epsilon}_{i}(X)\|^{2}+4\|T_{i}^{\epsilon}(X)-T^{\epsilon}(X)\|^{2}+4\|T^{\epsilon}(X)-\hat{T}^{\epsilon}(X)\|^{2}\right)\theta(n)\right]}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2\theta(n)\left(2\theta(n)+4M_{2}(\nu_{j})+4M_{2}(\mu)\right)}

\displaystyle\;\;\;\;\;+\sqrt{\mathbb{E}\left[\left(2\|\hat{T}^{\epsilon}_{i}(X)-T^{\epsilon}_{i}(X)\|^{2}+8\|T_{i}^{\epsilon}(X)\|^{2}+8\|T^{\epsilon}(X)\|^{2}+4\|T^{\epsilon}(X)-\hat{T}^{\epsilon}(X)\|^{2}\right)\theta(n)\right]}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{2\theta(n)\left(2\theta(n)+4M_{2}(\nu_{j})+4M_{2}(\mu)\right)}+\sqrt{\left(2\theta(n)+8M_{2}(\nu_{i})+8M_{2}(\mu)+4\theta(n)\right)\theta(n)}.

Applying the bound
M_{2}(\mu)\leq 2C_{G}^{2}q_{G}^{2}\sigma^{2}d
, which applies since
\mu
is
\sigma
subgaussian, and combining constants not depending on
\sigma
and
d
, we obtain:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\int\langle T_{j}^{\epsilon}-T^{\epsilon},T_{i}^{\epsilon}-T^{\epsilon}\rangle d\mu-\frac{1}{n}\sum_{k=n+1}^{2n}\langle\hat{T}_{j}^{\epsilon}(X_{k})-\hat{T}^{\epsilon}(X_{k}),\hat{T}_{i}^{\epsilon}(X_{k})-\hat{T}^{\epsilon}(X_{k})\rangle\bigg|\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tilde{J}d\sigma^{2}\max\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}},\sqrt{\theta(n)}+\sqrt{\theta(n)+\theta(n)^{2}}\}.

∎

Theorem
4.2
immediately follows from Lemmas
D.1
and
D.2
and the following lemma, which allows us to apply (expected) bounds between the entries of two matrices to the (expected) distance between their eigenvectors, which we have extracted from Corollary 2 in
[
66
]
:

Lemma D.3
.

Let
M\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times m}
be a positive-semidefinite matrix with eigenvalue 0 with unique eigenvector
\lambda_{*}\in\Delta^{m}
. Suppose that
\hat{M}\in\mathbb{R}^{m\times m}
is a random positive-semidefinite matrix such that

\mathbb{E}|[M]_{ij}-[\hat{M}]_{ij}|\leq\mathcal{Q},\;\text{for all }\;i,j=1,\dots,m.

(84)

Let
\hat{\lambda}:=\argmin_{\lambda\in\Delta^{m}}\lambda^{\top}\hat{M}\lambda
. Then:

\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{\lambda}-\lambda_{*}\|^{2}]\leq\frac{8m^{3}\mathcal{Q}}{\alpha_{2}},

where
\alpha_{2}
is the second smallest eigenvector of
M
.

Proof.

We decompose
\hat{\lambda}
as
\hat{\beta}\lambda_{*}+\hat{\lambda}_{\bot}
, where
\hat{\beta}\in\mathbb{R}
and
\hat{\lambda}_{\bot}
is orthogonal to
\lambda_{*}.

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{\lambda}-\lambda_{*}\|^{2}_{2}\right]=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{\lambda}-\hat{\beta}\lambda_{*}+\hat{\beta}\lambda_{*}-\lambda_{*}\|^{2}_{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{\lambda}-\hat{\beta}\lambda_{*}\|^{2}_{2}\right]+2\mathbb{E}\left[\|(\hat{\beta}-1)\lambda_{*}\|^{2}_{2}\right]

(85)

Observe that
\hat{\lambda}_{\bot}
is in the span of the eigenvectors
v_{2},...,v_{m}
of
M
, which we take to be ordered by their eigenvalues
0\leq\alpha_{2}\leq...\leq\alpha_{m}
. Furthermore, as
\lambda_{*}\in\Delta^{m}
is the unique eigenvector with eigenvalue
0
by assumption,
0<\alpha_{2}.
Then:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{\lambda}-\hat{\beta}\lambda_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\right]=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{\lambda}_{\bot}\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{j=2}^{m}|v_{j}^{\top}\hat{\lambda}_{\bot}|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha_{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{j=2}^{m}\alpha_{j}|v_{j}^{\top}\hat{\lambda}_{\bot}|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha_{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[|(\hat{\lambda}_{\bot})^{\top}M\hat{\lambda}_{\bot}\right]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\alpha_{2}}\mathbb{E}\left[|(\hat{\lambda})^{\top}M\hat{\lambda}|\right]

We bound:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\hat{\lambda}^{\top}M\hat{\lambda}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\hat{\lambda}^{\top}(M-\hat{M})\hat{\lambda}\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\hat{\lambda}^{\top}\hat{M}\hat{\lambda}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[|\hat{\lambda}^{\top}(M-\hat{M})\hat{\lambda}|\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\lambda_{*}^{\top}\hat{M}\lambda_{*}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[|\hat{\lambda}^{\top}(M-\hat{M})\hat{\lambda}|\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\lambda_{*}^{\top}(\hat{M}-M)\lambda_{*}\right]

where we used the minimality of
\hat{\lambda}
and the fact that
\lambda_{*}^{\top}M\lambda_{*}=0
. We may rewrite these as the following sums:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\bigg|\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}[\hat{\lambda}]_{i}[\hat{\lambda}]_{j}[M]_{ij}-[\hat{M}]_{ij}\bigg|\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}[\lambda_{*}]_{i}[\lambda_{*}]_{j}([\hat{M}]_{ij}-[M]_{ij})\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}\left[[\hat{\lambda}]_{i}[\hat{\lambda}]_{j}|[M]_{ij}-[\hat{M}]_{ij}|\right]+\sum_{i,j=1}^{m}[\lambda_{*}]_{i}[\lambda_{*}]_{j}\mathbb{E}\left[[\hat{M}]_{ij}-[M]_{ij}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2m^{2}\mathcal{Q},

(86)

where we used the triangle inequality, the fact that
\hat{\lambda},\lambda_{*}\in\Delta^{m}
and (
84
). This lets us bound (
85
) by:

\displaystyle\frac{4m^{2}}{\alpha_{2}}\mathcal{Q}+2\mathbb{E}\left[(\hat{\beta}-1)^{2}\|\lambda_{*}\|_{2}^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{4m^{2}}{\alpha_{2}}\mathcal{Q}+2\mathbb{E}\left[(\hat{\beta}-1)^{2}\right],

where we used the fact that
\|\lambda_{*}\|^{2}_{2}\leq 1
. Since
\hat{\lambda}=\hat{\beta}\lambda_{*}+\hat{\lambda}_{\bot}
with
\hat{\lambda},\lambda_{*}\in\Delta^{m}
, we have that:

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}[(\hat{\beta}-1)^{2}]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\sum_{j=1}^{m}[\hat{\lambda}_{\bot}]_{j}\right)^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq m\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{\lambda}_{\bot}\|_{2}^{2}]

by the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, and hence
\mathbb{E}[(1-\hat{\beta})^{2}]\leq m\mathbb{E}[\|\hat{\lambda}_{\bot}\|_{2}^{2}]\leq\frac{2m^{3}}{\alpha_{2}}\mathcal{Q}
by another application of (
86
). We conclude that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\|\hat{\lambda}-\lambda_{*}\|^{2}_{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\frac{4m^{2}}{\alpha_{2}}\mathcal{Q}+\frac{4m^{3}}{\alpha_{2}}\mathcal{Q}\leq\frac{8m^{3}}{\alpha_{2}}\mathcal{Q}.

∎

Proof of Theorem
4.2
:
Immediately follows from substituting the bounds from Lemmas
D.1
and
D.2
as
\mathcal{Q}
in Lemma
D.3
.∎

Proof of Corollary
4.3
:
The proof follows from the following result:

Theorem D.4
.

(Theorem 4 in
[
53
]
) Let
\Omega
be a bounded set in
\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
\epsilon>0
, and
\mu,\nu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\Omega)
. Then

\mathbb{E}\left[\|T_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}^{\epsilon}-T^{\epsilon}_{\hat{\mu}^{n}\rightarrow\hat{\nu}^{n}}\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}\right]<\frac{C_{\Omega,\epsilon}}{n}

where
C_{\Omega,\epsilon}
is a constant only depending on
|\Omega|
and
\epsilon.

By Theorem
D.4
, we bound

\mathbb{E}[\|T^{\epsilon}_{{\mu}\rightarrow{\nu}}-T^{\epsilon}_{{\hat{\mu}^{n}}\rightarrow{\hat{\nu}^{n}}}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mu)}\leq\frac{C_{\Omega,\epsilon}}{n}.

Similarly, we may bound

\mathbb{E}[\|T^{\epsilon}_{{\mu}\rightarrow{\mu}}-T^{\epsilon}_{{\hat{\mu}_{1}}\rightarrow{\hat{\mu}_{2}}}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mu)}]\leq\frac{3C_{\Omega,\epsilon}}{n}.

Hence we may apply Theorem
4.2
with
\theta(n)=\frac{1}{n}
. As
\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}+\frac{1}{n^{2}}}\leq\sqrt{\frac{2}{n}}
for
n\geq 1
, we may bound
\max\left\{\frac{1}{\sqrt{n}},\sqrt{\frac{1}{n}+\frac{1}{n^{2}}}\right\}\leq\frac{2}{\sqrt{n}}.
The second moments of
\nu_{1},...,\nu_{m},\mu
are uniformly bounded by
|\Omega|^{2}
, and hence we may collapse the various constants into a single constant
\tilde{C}_{\Omega,\epsilon}
.
∎

Proof of Corollary
4.4
:
It is well known that
c
-strongly log-concave measures are
\frac{L}{\sqrt{c}}
-subgaussian for some universal constant
L
(see e.g., Theorem 5 in
[
64
]
). Similarly to Corollary
4.3
, the proof follows from the following result:

Theorem D.5
.

(Theorem 7 in
[
64
]
) Let
\mu
be
\sigma
-subgaussian and let
\nu
be
c
-strongly log concave with
\mathbb{E}(\nu)=0
. Then there exists an estimator
\mathcal{L}
such that:

\mathbb{E}\left[\|T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}-\mathcal{L}(\hat{\mu}^{n},\hat{\nu}^{n})\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}\right]\leq\frac{K_{d,\sigma,\epsilon,c}}{n^{1/3}},

where
K_{d,\sigma,\epsilon,c}
is a constant depending on
c,\sigma,d
and
\epsilon.

Since
\mu
is a critical point of
F_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}^{\epsilon}
, it is
\frac{L}{\sqrt{c}}
-subgaussian by Proposition
2.7
, and hence the constant factors only depend on
c,d
and
\epsilon
.
∎

D.2
Proof of Proposition
4.5

To establish Proposition
4.5
, we will require the following technical results:

Lemma D.6
.

(Lemma A.1 in
[
65
]
) Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
be bounded, and let
\mu,\nu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\Omega)
. Then:

\|T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}(x)-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu}(y)\|\leq M_{\Omega,\epsilon}\|x-y\|,

for all
x,y\in\Omega
, where
M_{\Omega,\epsilon}=\frac{4|\Omega|^{2}}{\epsilon}
.

Proposition D.7
.

(Proposition 4 in
[
34
]
and Corollary 3.3 in
[
22
]
) Let
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
be bounded, and let
\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\nu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\Omega)
. Then:

\|T^{\epsilon}_{\nu\rightarrow\mu_{1}}-T^{\epsilon}_{\nu\rightarrow\mu_{2}}\|_{L^{2}(\nu)}\leq K_{\Omega,\epsilon}OT_{2}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})

(87)

\|T^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{1}\rightarrow\nu}-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu_{2}\rightarrow\nu}\|_{L^{2}(\mu_{1})}\leq K_{\Omega,\epsilon}OT_{2}(\mu_{1},\mu_{2})

(88)

where
K_{\Omega,\epsilon}=\frac{3^{1/2}|\Omega|}{\epsilon^{1/2}}+1
.

From Proposition
D.7
we obtain the following lemma:

Lemma D.8
.

Suppose
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
is bounded. Let
A_{\mu}^{\epsilon}
and
S_{\mu}^{\epsilon}
be defined as in Proposition
4.1
. Then for any
1\leq i,j\leq m
:

|[A^{\epsilon}_{\mu}]_{ij}-[A_{\rho}^{\epsilon}]_{ij}|\leq H_{\Omega,\epsilon}OT_{2}(\rho,\mu)

|[S^{\epsilon}_{\mu}]_{ij}-[S_{\rho}^{\epsilon}]_{ij}|\leq H_{\Omega,\epsilon}OT_{2}(\mu,\rho)

where
H_{\Omega,\epsilon}
depends on
|\Omega|
and
\epsilon.

Proof.

We begin by bounding
|[A_{\mu}^{\epsilon}]_{ij}-[A_{\rho}^{\epsilon}]_{ij}|
for any
1\leq i,j\leq m
:

\displaystyle|[A_{\mu}^{\epsilon}]_{ij}-[A_{\rho}^{\epsilon}]_{ij}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\langle Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}},Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{j}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu)}-\langle Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{i}},Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\rho)}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\langle Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}},Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{j}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu)}-\langle Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}},Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu)}|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle|\langle Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}},Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu)}-\langle Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}},Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\rho)}|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle|\langle Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}},Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\rho)}-\langle Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{i}},Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\rho)}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\langle Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}},T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{j}}-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu)}|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle|\langle Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}},Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu)}-\langle Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}},Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\rho)}|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle|\langle T_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}}-T_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{i}},Id-T_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\rho)}|

(89)

where we applied the triangle inequality. We bound the first term in (
89
):

\displaystyle|\langle Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}},T_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{j}}^{\epsilon}-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu)}|

\displaystyle\leq\|Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}}\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}\|T_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{j}}^{\epsilon}-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}

\displaystyle\leq|\Omega|\|T_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{j}}^{\epsilon}-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}

\displaystyle\leq|\Omega|K_{\Omega,\epsilon}OT_{2}(\mu,\rho),

where we applied the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, the diameter bound on
\Omega
and (
88
) to control
\|T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{j}}-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}
. The third term can be bounded identically.

To bound the second term, we first observe that, by Lemma
D.6
,
T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}}
and
T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}
are
M_{\Omega,\epsilon}
-Lipschitz vector-valued maps from
\Omega
to the convex hull of
\Omega.

As
\Omega
is bounded,
x\mapsto\langle x-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}}(x),x-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(x)\rangle
is
2|\Omega|(1+M_{\Omega,\epsilon})
-Lipschitz as a function of
x\in\Omega
. Indeed, let
x,y\in\Omega
. Then:

\displaystyle|\langle x-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}}(x),x-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(x)\rangle-\langle y-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}}(y),y-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(y)\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\langle x-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}}(x),x-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(x)\rangle-\langle x-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}}(x),y-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(y)\rangle|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle|\langle x-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}}(x),y-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(y)\rangle-\langle y-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}}(y),y-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(y)\rangle|

(90)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\langle x-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}}(x),x-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(x)-y+T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(y)\rangle|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle|\langle x-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}}(x)-y+T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}}(y),y-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(y)\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|x-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}}(x)\|\|x-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(x)-y+T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(y)\|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\|x-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}}(x)-y+T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}}(y)\|\|y-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(y)\|

(91)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\Omega|\left(\|x-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}}(x)-y+T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}}(y)\|+\|x-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(x)-y+T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(y)\|\right)

(92)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\Omega|\left(2\|x-y\|+\|T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}}(x)-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}}(y)\|+\|T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(x)-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(y)\|\right)

(93)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2|\Omega|(1+M_{\Omega,\epsilon})\|x-y\|.

(94)

In (
90
) we applied the triangle inequality, in (
91
) the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, in (
92
) we used the bounds
\|x-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}}(x)\|,\|y-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(y)\|\leq|\Omega|
, in (
93
) the triangle inequality and finally in (
94
) we applied Lemma
D.6
. Since
\langle Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}},Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}\rangle
is
2|\Omega|(1+M_{\Omega,\epsilon})
-Lipschitz, we may apply Kantorovich-Rubinstein duality for the Wasserstein-1 distance (see e.g., 5.16 in
[
63
]
) to produce the bound:

\displaystyle|\langle Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}},Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu)}-\langle Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}},Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\rho)}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2|\Omega|(1+M_{\Omega,\epsilon})OT_{1}(\mu,\rho)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2|\Omega|(1+M_{\Omega,\epsilon})OT_{2}(\mu,\rho)

where in the last line we applied Jensen’s inequality. Combining the bounds on these three terms, we may conclude that
|[A_{\mu}^{\epsilon}]_{ij}-[A_{\rho}^{\epsilon}]_{ij}|\leq 2|\Omega|(M_{\Omega,\epsilon}+K_{\Omega,\epsilon}+1)OT_{2}(\mu,\rho)
.

We now treat the Sinkhorn case. For any
\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\Omega)
and
1\leq i,j\leq m
, we have:

\displaystyle|[S_{\mu}^{\epsilon}]_{ij}-[S_{\rho}^{\epsilon}]_{ij}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\langle T_{\mu\rightarrow\mu}^{\epsilon}-T_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}}^{\epsilon},T_{\mu\rightarrow\mu}^{\epsilon}-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{j}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu)}-\langle T_{\rho\rightarrow\rho}^{\epsilon}-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{i}},T_{\rho\rightarrow\rho}^{\epsilon}-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\rho)}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\langle T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\mu}-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}},T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\mu}-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{j}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu)}-\langle T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\rho}-T^{\epsilon}_{{\mu}\rightarrow{\nu_{i}}},T^{\epsilon}_{{\mu}\rightarrow{\mu}}-T^{\epsilon}_{{\mu}\rightarrow{\nu_{j}}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu)}|

(95)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle|\langle T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\rho}-T^{\epsilon}_{{\mu}\rightarrow{\nu_{i}}},T^{\epsilon}_{{\mu}\rightarrow{\mu}}-T^{\epsilon}_{{\mu}\rightarrow{\nu_{j}}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu)}-\langle T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\rho}-T^{\epsilon}_{{\mu}\rightarrow{\nu_{i}}},T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}-T^{\epsilon}_{{\mu}\rightarrow{\nu_{j}}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu)}|

(96)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle|\langle T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}},T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}-T^{\epsilon}_{{\mu}\rightarrow{\nu_{j}}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu)}-\langle T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}-T^{\epsilon}_{{\mu}\rightarrow{\nu_{i}}},T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}-T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\nu_{j}}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu)}|

(97)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle|\langle T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}-T^{\epsilon}_{{\mu}\rightarrow{\nu_{i}}},T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}-T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\nu_{j}}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu)}-\langle T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}-T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\nu_{i}}},T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}-T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\nu_{j}}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu)}|

(98)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle|\langle T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}-T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\nu_{i}}},T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}-T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\nu_{j}}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu)}-\langle T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}-T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\nu_{i}}},T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}-T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\nu_{j}}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\rho)}|

(99)

We first bound (
95
):

\displaystyle|\langle T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\mu}-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}},T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\mu}-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{j}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu)}-\langle T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\rho}-T^{\epsilon}_{{\mu}\rightarrow{\nu_{i}}},T^{\epsilon}_{{\mu}\rightarrow{\mu}}-T^{\epsilon}_{{\mu}\rightarrow{\nu_{j}}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu)}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\langle T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\mu}-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\rho},T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\mu}-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{j}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu)}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|T^{\epsilon}_{{\mu}\rightarrow{\mu}}-T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}\|T^{\epsilon}_{{\mu}\rightarrow{\mu}}-T^{\epsilon}_{{\mu}\rightarrow{\nu_{j}}}\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|T^{\epsilon}_{{\mu}\rightarrow{\mu}}-T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}|\Omega|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(\|T^{\epsilon}_{{\mu}\rightarrow{\mu}}-T^{\epsilon}_{{\mu}\rightarrow{\rho}}\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}+\|T^{\epsilon}_{{\mu}\rightarrow{\rho}}-T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}\|_{L^{2}(\mu)})|\Omega|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2K_{\Omega,\epsilon}|\Omega|OT_{2}(\mu,\rho),

where we applied the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality, the fact that the image of
T^{\epsilon}_{{\mu}\rightarrow{\mu}}
and
T^{\epsilon}_{{\mu}\rightarrow{\nu_{j}}}
are contained in
\Omega
, followed by (
87
) and (
88
). The same bound applies to (
96
).

A similar argument applies to (
97
):

\displaystyle|\langle T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}},T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}-T^{\epsilon}_{{\mu}\rightarrow{\nu_{j}}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu)}-\langle T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}-T^{\epsilon}_{{\mu}\rightarrow{\nu_{i}}},T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}-T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\nu_{j}}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu)}|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\langle T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}},T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\nu_{j}}}-T^{\epsilon}_{{\mu}\rightarrow{\nu_{j}}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu)}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}-T^{\epsilon}_{{\mu}\rightarrow{\nu_{i}}}\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}\|T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\nu_{j}}}-T^{\epsilon}_{{\mu}\rightarrow{\nu_{j}}}\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\Omega|K_{\Omega,\epsilon}OT_{2}(\mu,\rho)

where we applied (
88
) and the fact that
\|T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}(x)-T^{\epsilon}_{{\mu}\rightarrow{\nu_{i}}}(x)\|\leq|\Omega|
for all
x\in\Omega
. The same bound applies to (
98
).

Finally, to bound (
99
), we observe that
x\rightarrow\langle T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}(x)-T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\nu_{i}}}(x),T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}(x)-T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\nu_{j}}}(x)\rangle
is
4|\Omega|M_{\Omega,\epsilon}
-Lipschitz:

\displaystyle|\langle T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}(x)-T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\nu_{i}}}(x),T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}(x)-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(x)\rangle-\langle T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}(y)-T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\nu_{i}}}(y),T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}(y)-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(y)\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\langle T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}(x)-T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\nu_{i}}}(x),T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}(x)-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(x)\rangle-\langle T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}(x)-T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\nu_{i}}}(x),T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}(y)-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(y)\rangle|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle|\langle T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}(x)-T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\nu_{i}}}(x),T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}(y)-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(y)\rangle-\langle T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}(y)-T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\nu_{i}}}(y),T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}(y)-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(y)\rangle|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle|\langle T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}(x)-T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\nu_{i}}}(x),T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}(x)-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(x)-T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}(y)+T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(y)\rangle|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle|\langle T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}(x)-T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\nu_{i}}}(x)-T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}(y)+T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\nu_{i}}}(y),T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}(y)-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(y)\rangle|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}(x)-T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\nu_{i}}}(x)\|\|T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}(x)-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(x)-T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}(y)+T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(y)\|

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\|T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}(x)-T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\nu_{i}}}(x)-T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}(y)+T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\nu_{i}}}(y)\|\|T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}(y)-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(y)\|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\Omega|\left(\|T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}(x)-T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\nu_{i}}}(x)-T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}(y)+T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\nu_{i}}}(y)\|+\|T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}(x)-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(x)-T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}(y)+T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(y)\|\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\Omega|\left(2\|T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}(x)-T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}(y)\|+\|T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\nu_{i}}}(x)-T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\nu_{i}}}(y)\|+\|T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(x)-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}(y)\|\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4|\Omega|M_{\Omega,\epsilon}\|x-y\|,

where we applied Lemma
D.6
in the final line. Thus we may apply Kantorovich-Rubinstein duality once more to conclude that

\displaystyle|\langle T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}-T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\nu_{i}}},T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu)}-\langle T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}-T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\nu_{i}}},T^{\epsilon}_{{\rho}\rightarrow{\rho}}-T^{\epsilon}_{\rho\rightarrow\nu_{j}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\rho)}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4|\Omega|M_{\Omega,\epsilon}OT_{1}(\mu,\rho)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4|\Omega|M_{\Omega,\epsilon}OT_{2}(\mu,\rho).

Combining the bounds on (
95
)-(
99
), we have that:

|[S_{\mu}^{\epsilon}]_{ij}-[S_{\rho}^{\epsilon}]_{ij}|\leq|\Omega|(6K_{\Omega,\epsilon}+4M_{\Omega,\epsilon})OT_{2}(\mu,\rho).

Taking the maximum of these to be
H_{\Omega,\epsilon}
, we conclude.
∎

Proof of Proposition
4.5
:
We apply Lemma
D.3
with
M=A^{\epsilon}_{\mu}
and
\hat{M}=A^{\epsilon}_{\rho}
(note this is deterministic) and can take
\mathcal{Q}=H_{\Omega,\epsilon}OT_{2}(\mu,\rho)
by Lemma
D.8
. The same holds for
S^{\epsilon}_{\mu}
. ∎

Appendix E
Implementation Details

E.1
Synthesis Implementation

Input:
Initial measure
\mu_{0}\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\Omega)
, functional
\phi:\mathcal{P}_{2}(\Omega)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
, step-size
\eta>0
, iterations
k
.

1

\mathcal{T}_{[0:0]}:=\mathcal{T}_{0}\leftarrow Id-\eta\nabla\delta\phi[\mu_{0}]
;

//
Compute Wasserstein-2 gradient of
\phi
at
\mu_{0}
;

\mu_{1}\leftarrow[\mathcal{T}_{[0:0]}]_{\#}(\mu_{0})
;

//
Transport
\mu_{0}
according to
\mathcal{T}_{[0:0]}

2

for

0\leq i\leq k-1

do

3

\mathcal{T}_{i}\leftarrow Id-\eta\nabla\delta\phi[\mu_{i}]
;

//
Compute Wasserstein-2 gradient of
\phi
at
\mu_{i}

\mathcal{T}_{[0:i]}\leftarrow\mathcal{T}_{i}\circ\mathcal{T}_{[0:i-1]}
;

//
Update by composing maps

\mu_{i+1}\leftarrow[\mathcal{T}_{[0:i]}]_{\#}(\mu_{0})
;

//
Transport
\mu_{0}
according to composed map

Output:

\mu_{k}
.

Algorithm 4

Wasserstein gradient descent for
\phi
(WGD)

We detail the implementation of the free support synthesis algorithm used in Section
4.1
(see Algorithm
4
for pseudocode). For the case of
F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}
, this corresponds to a modified version of Algorithm 3 in
[
21
]
, without the density update step. More generally, we may view Algorithm
4
as a forward Euler discretization of the Wasserstein-2 gradient flow
[
2
]
for a functional
\phi:\mathcal{P}_{2}(\Omega)\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
with some fixed step-size
\eta
. It is also implemented for
F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}
for the popular optimal transport package Python Optimal Transport (POT)
[
27
]
. See Theorem 4.1 in
[
57
]
for a proof of convergence in the bounded setting.

We use Algorithm
4
to compute (approximate) critical points of
\mathcal{F}^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}
. In each trial, we construct
\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n}
by sampling
n=20000
samples according to
\nu_{j}
. We construct
\mu_{0}
by sampling an empirical measure (on
n=20000
samples) from
Unif([0,1]^{5})
. We apply Algorithm
4
to
F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}
with initial measure
\mu_{0}
and
\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n}:=\{\hat{\nu}_{j}^{n}\}_{j=1}^{m}
, with stopping criterion
\|Id-\eta\nabla\delta F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\hat{\mathcal{V}}^{n}}[\mu_{i}]\|_{L^{2}(\mu_{i})}<10^{-7}
. We denote the output by
\mu^{*}
.

E.2
Sample Complexity Experiment Implementation Details

1-Dimensional Gaussians:
We build a random set of reference measures

\mathcal{V}=\{\mathcal{N}(M_{1},\sigma^{2}_{1}),\mathcal{N}(M_{2},\sigma^{2}_{2}),...,\mathcal{N}(M_{m},\sigma^{2}_{m})\}

for
m\geq 1
where
M_{j}
are i.i.d.
\sim\mbox{Unif}([a_{j},b_{j}])
and
\sigma_{i}
i.i.d.
\sim\mbox{Unif}([c_{j},d_{j}])
. We then sample
\tilde{\lambda}_{1},\tilde{\lambda}_{2},...,\tilde{\lambda}_{m}
i.i.d.
\sim\mbox{Unif}([0,1])
, and define
\lambda_{j}:=\tilde{\lambda}_{j}/\sum_{i=1}^{m}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}
for all
j
. We then compute the exact mean
\bar{M}
and variance
S
of the true minimizer
\argmin_{\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R})}F_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}^{\epsilon}(\mu)
, which is known in closed form via Theorem 2 in
[
30
]
(we solve for the variance numerically using
scipy.optimize least
\_
squares
). We then run Algorithm
1
over
n
samples, with
\mu=\mathcal{N}(\bar{M},S^{2})
and
\mathcal{V}
,
\lambda
and
\epsilon
chosen as above to recover a set of estimated coefficients
\hat{\lambda}^{n}
. We compute the
\ell^{2}
error between
\hat{\lambda}^{n}
and
\lambda
.

In the case of
m=2
, we sample
M_{1}\sim\mbox{Unif}([0,2])
,
M_{2}\sim\mbox{Unif}([3,5])
and
\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}\sim\mbox{Unif}([2,4])
. In the case of
m=3
, we sample
M_{1},M_{2},\sigma_{1},\sigma_{2}
as before, and sample
M_{3}\sim\mbox{Unif}([-2,0])
and
\sigma_{3}\sim\mbox{Unif}([2,4]).
We found that the
\ell^{2}
-distance between the coefficients essentially does not decay in
n
(top right). This is due to the fact that in one dimension, the matrix
A^{\epsilon}_{\mu}
is often singular, which violates the conditions in Theorem
4.2
, leading to problems with non-unique recovery of coefficients. Despite this, we empirically show that the expected
OT_{2}^{2}
-error between the barycenters associated to
\lambda
and
\hat{\lambda}^{n}
rapidly decays as
n
increases, implying successful recovery of a
valid
set of barycentric coordinates even when they are non-unique (see Appendix
F
).

5-Dimensional Gaussians:
We work with a fixed, random choice of
\lambda
, which we obtain by sampling
\tilde{\lambda}_{1},\tilde{\lambda}_{2},...,\tilde{\lambda}_{m}
i.i.d.
\sim\mbox{Unif}([0,1])
, and define
\lambda_{j}:=\tilde{\lambda}_{j}/\sum_{i=1}^{m}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}
. For each trial, we set
\epsilon=1
, and generate each Gaussian
\nu_{j}:=\mathcal{N}(M_{j},A_{j})
as follows. We sample
M_{1}\sim\mbox{Unif}([0.2,0.4]^{5}),M_{2}\sim\mbox{Unif}([0.4,0.6]^{5})
and
M_{3}\sim\mbox{Unif}([0.6,0.8]^{5})
.

For each
j
, we generate covariance matrix
\Sigma_{j}=Rv_{j}v_{j}^{\top}+0.7*Id
, where
v_{j}\in\mathbb{R}^{5}
is generated by sampling
[v_{j}]_{i}\sim Unif([0.2,0.5])
for
1\leq i\leq 5
and
R
is a uniformly random
d
-dimensional rotation matrix. We work with a fixed choice of coefficients
\lambda
across all trials, which we generate by sampling
\tilde{\lambda_{i}}\sim\mbox{Unif}([0,1])
and setting
\lambda_{i}=\tilde{\lambda}_{i}/\sum_{j=1}^{m}\tilde{\lambda}_{j}
. Unlike the 1D case, we no longer have access to the closed-form solution for the entropic barycenter. We implement a free support synthesis algorithm (see details in Appendix
E.1
) to compute an approximate fixed point
\mu^{*}
. We then sample
2n
samples from
\mu^{*}
and resample
n
samples from
\{\nu_{j}\}_{j=1}^{m}
and use these as inputs for Algorithm
1
, as we let
n
range over
[10,20,40,...,10240]
.

5-Dimensional Cubes
: We work with a fixed, random choice of
\lambda
, which we obtain by sampling
\tilde{\lambda}_{1},\tilde{\lambda}_{2},...,\tilde{\lambda}_{m}
i.i.d.
\sim\mbox{Unif}([0,1])
, and define
\lambda_{j}:=\tilde{\lambda}_{j}/\sum_{i=1}^{m}\tilde{\lambda}_{i}
. For each trial, we set
\epsilon=1
, and generate our reference measures using random translations of
Unif([0,2]^{5}).
Specifically, we let
\nu_{j}=Unif([0,2]^{5}+M_{j}])
where
M_{1}\sim\mbox{Unif}([0.2,0.4]^{5}),M_{2}\sim\mbox{Unif}([0.4,0.6]^{5})
and
M_{3}\sim\mbox{Unif}([0.6,0.8]^{5})
.

We solve the minimization problem
\min_{\lambda\in\Delta^{3}}\lambda^{\top}\hat{A}_{\mu^{*}}^{\epsilon}\lambda
using the
cp.Minimize
function from the convex optimization module cvx.py, and denote the output
\hat{\lambda}^{n}
.

E.3
Point Cloud Classification Implementation Details

Analysis for the barycenter functional:
We compute the barycentric coordinates for the unregularized barycenter functional by computing a set of maps
\{T^{0}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{j}}\}_{j=1}^{m}
, where
T^{0}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{j}}:=\mathbb{E}_{\zeta_{j}}[Y|X=x]
is the conditional expectation of an optimal coupling
\zeta_{j}\in\Pi(\mu,\nu_{j})
for all
1\leq j\leq m
, which we use as inputs to define a matrix
A^{0}_{\mu}
, defined by
[A_{\mu}^{0}]_{ij}=\langle Id-T^{0}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}},Id-T^{0}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{j}}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu)}
. We solve the corresponding optimization problem
\min_{\lambda\in\Delta^{m}}\lambda^{\top}A_{\mu}^{0}\lambda
.

Analysis for the doubly-regularized functional:
For the doubly regularized problem, we must assume that
\mu
admits a density
P_{\mu}
. Then the corresponding matrix is
D^{\epsilon,\tau}_{\mu}
, defined by
[D_{\mu}^{\epsilon,\tau}]_{ij}=\langle Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{i}}+\tau\nabla\log P_{\mu},Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{j}}+\tau\nabla\log P_{\mu}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu)}
; see Appendix
G
for details. In practice, we estimate
\nabla\log P_{\mu}
using the
\nu
-method implemented in
[
69
]
. Our choice of kernel is the curl-free inverse multiquadric kernel with bandwidth 5.0, and regularization strength
10^{-5}
(see
[
69
]
and the associated implementation for details). We additionally convolved
\mu
with isotropic Gaussian noise with standard deviation
0.05
, which empirically improved the performance of our method.

Appendix F
Recovery with Non-Unique Barycentric Coordinates

We further study the analysis problem for three one-dimensional Gaussians. In Section
4.1
, we showed that coefficient recovery fails in this setting. We claim this is due to the fact that there are multiple sets of coefficients giving rise to the same barycenter, i.e.,
A_{\mu}^{\epsilon}
is often singular.

Let
\mu^{*}
be the barycenter with respect to the original coefficients
\lambda
and
\hat{\mu}^{n}
be the barycenter with the recovered coefficients
\hat{\lambda}^{n}
as
n
ranges over the same set of values. To estimate
\mathbb{E}[OT_{2}^{2}(\mu^{*},\hat{\mu}^{n})]
, we estimate the mean and variance of
\hat{\mu}^{n}
(denoted
\hat{m},\hat{\sigma}
) and applying the closed-form formula for the optimal transport distance between two Gaussians
[
9
]
, which specializes in this case to
OT_{2}^{2}(\mu^{*},\hat{\mu}^{n})=(\bar{\mu}-\hat{m})^{2}+S^{2}+\hat{\sigma}^{2}-2\hat{\sigma}S
. We average this quantity over 100 trials to estimate
\mathbb{E}[OT_{2}^{2}(\mu^{*},\hat{\mu}^{n})]
. We plot our results in Figure
2
, and see that
\mathbb{E}[OT_{2}^{2}(\mu^{*},\hat{\mu}^{n})]
decays faster than
n^{-1}
. Hence from the perspective of barycentric coordinates, our method is successful even in the case of non-unique recovery.

Figure 2
:
Average
\log
squared-
OT_{2}
-loss for three random 1D Gaussian measures with random weights,
\epsilon=2
.

Appendix G
Derivation of Quadratic Program for Doubly Regularized Functional

In this section, we derive the form of the quadratic program used for solving the analysis problem for the
(\epsilon,\tau)
-doubly-regularized barycenter functional,
\mathcal{D}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}^{\epsilon,\tau}:=F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}+\tau H(\mu)
, under the assumption that the measure
\mu
being analyzed is a critical point of the doubly-regularized functional. Here,
H
denotes the negative differential entropy:

H(\mu):=\Bigg\{\begin{array}[]{lr}\displaystyle\int_{\Omega}\log\left(P_{\mu}(x)\right)P_{\mu}(x)dx,\text{if}\;\mu\;\text{is absolutely continuous with respect to the Lebesgue measure on $\Omega$}\\
\;+\infty,\;\text{otherwise}\end{array}

where
P_{\mu}
denotes the density of
\mu
and
\Omega\subset\mathbb{R}^{d}
.

Let
\mu\in\mathcal{P}_{2}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
be absolutely continuous with connected support. For convenience, we will also assume that
\mu
has
C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{d})
density
P_{\mu}
. Then we compute:

\displaystyle\delta\mathcal{D}^{\epsilon,\tau}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu)

\displaystyle=\delta F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu)+\delta\tau H(\mu)

\displaystyle=\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}f^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{j}}+\tau\log P_{\mu}.

Since
\mu
is a critical point, we have that
\|\nabla\delta\mathcal{D}^{\epsilon,\tau}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu)\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mu)}=0
, which implies that:

\displaystyle Id-\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{j}}+\tau\nabla\log P_{\mu}=0

\mu
-almost everywhere. We may then compute:

\displaystyle\|\nabla\delta\mathcal{D}^{\epsilon,\tau}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}(\mu)\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}^{2}

\displaystyle=\|Id-\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{j}}+\tau\nabla\log P_{\mu}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mu)}

\displaystyle=\int\langle Id-\sum_{j=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{j}}+\tau\nabla\log P_{\mu},Id-\sum_{k=1}^{m}\lambda_{k}T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{k}}+\tau\nabla\log P_{\mu}\rangle d\mu

\displaystyle=\int\sum_{j,k=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\lambda_{k}\langle Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{j}}+\tau\nabla\log P_{\mu},Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{k}}+\tau\nabla\log P_{\mu}\rangle d\mu

\displaystyle=\sum_{j,k=1}^{m}\lambda_{j}\lambda_{k}[D^{\epsilon,\tau}_{\mu}]_{jk}=\lambda^{\top}D^{\epsilon,\tau}_{\mu}\lambda,

where
D^{\epsilon,\tau}_{\mu}
is defined by
[D^{\epsilon,\tau}_{\mu}]_{jk}:=\langle Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{j}}+\tau\nabla\log P_{\mu},Id-T^{\epsilon}_{\mu\rightarrow\nu_{k}}+\tau\nabla\log P_{\mu}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mu)}
. Hence if
\mu
is a critical point for
\mathcal{D}^{\epsilon,\tau}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}
, then solving the analysis problem is equivalent to solving
0=\lambda^{\top}D^{\epsilon,\tau}_{\mu}\lambda
for
\lambda\in\Delta^{m}
. Note that the assumed smoothness of
P_{\mu}
and the guaranteed smoothness of the potentials, together with the connectedness of
\texttt{supp}(\mu)
imply the reverse implication as well:
\|\nabla\delta\mathcal{D}_{\lambda,V}^{\epsilon,\tau}(\mu)\|_{L^{2}(\mu)}=0
implies
\mu
is a critical point.

Appendix H
Application: Point Cloud Completion

We include a preliminary demonstration of an application of the synthesis and analysis methods to point cloud completion. We work with the PointCloud-C data set
[
51
]
which consists of point cloud representations of objects corrupted by various regimes of noise and occlusions. In this demonstration, we use data from the
clean
,
dropout
\_
global
\_
4
and
dropout
\_
local
\_
4
datasets. The
clean
dataset consists of point clouds with
1024
points. The
dropout
\_
global
\_
4
dataset is obtained by selecting point clouds from the
clean
dataset and (uniformly) randomly removing
75\%
of the data points. The
dropout
\_
local
\_
4
dataset by selecting a random set of points
x_{1},x_{2},...,x_{\ell}
(
\ell\leq 8
) and removing the
N_{i}
nearest points to each
x_{i}
, where
\sum_{i=1}^{\ell}N_{i}=500
(see
[
51
]
for more details). We give some examples in Figure
3
. The object of point cloud completion is to take a partial point cloud and to generate a new point cloud which as closely as possible matches the original point cloud. Unlike most methods in the literature, our method does not require explicit training.

Figure 3
:
(Left to right) Point clouds from
clean
,
dropout
\_
global
\_
4
and
local
\_
dropout
\_
4
.

Out method proceeds as follows. We let
\mu_{0}
be the uniform measure on a corrupted point cloud. We then select a set of random clean point clouds
\nu_{1},...,\nu_{5}
from the same class of objects. We solve the analysis problem for
\mu_{0}
with Algorithm
1
, producing a set of coefficients
\lambda\in\Delta^{5}
. We then use these coefficients and reference measures to synthesize a new point cloud
\bar{\mu}_{0}
, using Algorithm
4
, initialized at an empirical measure (sampled from a uniform measure on the 3D cube) supported on
1024
points. We remark that this method is similar to one developed in
[
66
]
for image completion, with a key difference being that their method operates in a fixed-support setting, as opposed to free-support like ours.

Figure 4
:
(Top left) Global dropout,
F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}
reconstruction, (top right) global dropout
S^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}
reconstruction, (bottom left) local dropout,
F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}
reconstruction, (bottom right) local dropout
S^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}
reconstruction.
OT_{2}
-cost denotes the
OT_{2}
distance between the (uniform measures on the) clean point cloud and the reconstruction.

In Figure
4
we show results for our completion method using both
F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}
and
S^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}
. We see that in all cases our methods are able to produce a completed point cloud that roughly match the original. The reconstructions using
F^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}
appear to be more singular than the original, whereas
S^{\epsilon}_{\lambda,\mathcal{V}}
appear to be more diffuse. However some fine details of the original point cloud are not recovered, such as the second turbine on the left wing.

To give a quantitative comparison, we compute the
OT_{2}
-distance between the (uniform measures on the) clean point cloud and the reconstruction. We see that the Sinkhorn reconstruction method performs slightly better in both cases with respect to this metric.
